- **P6-4a territory and correction contract:** the synchronous soft-window path persists one immutable mutation journal, but currently follows that durability boundary with one selected producer-workspace object, manifest, checkpoint, and selector advance per journal record. Each admission also linearly searches, re-sorts, and re-verifies the growing retained-task vector, making the 10,000-record commit path both durability-amplified and effectively quadratic. Recovery reads each selected producer object back through `NativeIndexRecoveredTaskSinkV1`; servicing then reloads the same journal once per record. The format-compatible correction persists exactly one existing AITK-v1 `Reconcile` task per journal. Its in-memory continuation processes a bounded page of exact record operations from one journal read, preserves each record's derived operation identity and publication sequence, and acknowledges the reconcile task only after the final record. Restart may replay from record zero because record operation identities remain idempotent; no persistent bytes, selected-checkpoint shape, workspace rotation rule, or migration contract changes.
- **P6-4b territory and correction contract:** `NativeIndexParserExecutorV1` is installed only by the migration-qualified v4 runtime and is the sole caller of `parse_native_corrected`; the live v3 `IndexingPipeline`, public native-parser API, and existing parser compatibility specs call `parse_native` and must remain behaviorally unchanged. Corrected raw JSON currently applies the frozen node/depth/member/scalar policy only after constructing the complete canonical tree. Corrected Office/ODF dispatch currently reuses legacy ZIP readers whose `read_to_string` calls have no declared-size, streaming, per-entry, or cumulative expansion bound, so a tiny compressed body can materially exceed both its parser reservation and process memory. P6-4b will first retain allocator-level failing evidence in an isolated integration binary, then add corrected-only during-parse JSON limits and cumulative ZIP expansion limits derived from the frozen native response policy. Resource-limit failures remain deterministic policy outcomes, malformed archives remain deterministic malformed outcomes, host allocation failures remain typed host failures, and no persistent bytes, parser resolution semantics, dependency fingerprint, or legacy parser result changes.

## P6-4b Native Parser Resource Proof

- **Red evidence:** the isolated counting-allocator target reproduced 100,696,961 bytes of peak allocator growth for a 24 MiB deflated DOCX entry and 50,821,057 bytes for high-node JSON before the correction. Both inputs returned deterministic-unindexable only after the old implementation had materialized the amplified representation.
- **Correction:** corrected-only Office/ODF routing now uses declared-size prechecks plus bounded 16 KiB streaming reads under one cumulative archive budget equal to four times the frozen native response limit. Extracted document text, Office properties, ODF metadata, and ODF keyword arrays enforce scalar, response, node, and member limits while being built. Corrected JSON uses a policy-aware Serde seed/visitor that stops at the frozen node, depth, member, and scalar boundary during deserialization. Corrected native output converts directly into canonical values instead of serializing and reparsing a second full tree. Legacy `parse_native`, v3 routing, result semantics, and all 118 legacy parser regressions remain unchanged.
- **Accounting correction:** the one-entry-map adversary proved that `size_of::<CanonicalConfigValueV1>()` materially undercounts retained `BTreeMap` nodes. ## P6-4c Producer Commit and Native Cadence Proof

- **Existing-chunk HTTP proof:** an exact-source debug server on a disposable loopback database committed one 5,242,880,000-byte logical file described by 20,000 references to one already-present 256 KiB chunk. The 1,520,283-byte manifest supplied exact size and full content hash. Commit returned HTTP 200 in 0.165816 seconds; the engine completed in 137 ms with 20,310 us of chunk-metadata lookup, zero chunk-body reads, zero sniff reads, 94 ms validation, and 41 ms namespace publication. Twenty-four additional distinct files produced 0.145756/0.162418/0.176837-second minimum/average/maximum commit latency with no failure.
- **Responsiveness and memory:** 83 health requests sampled during those commits had zero failures and 0.000660/0.000981/0.001321-second minimum/average/maximum latency. Server RSS moved from 85,780 KiB to 106,156 KiB while retaining metadata for 25 logical 5.24 GB files, then reopened at 60,260 KiB. This run observed neither health starvation nor logical-byte-proportional memory growth.
- **Restart/read/integrity proof:** byte ranges at the first and last 32 bytes returned HTTP 206 and exact bytes before restart; the last range remained exact after clean restart. The restarted listing retained all 25 files and the 5,242,880,000-byte target. Startup log timestamps span about 596 ms. Offline verification before and after restart reports 462 valid entries, zero corrupt headers/hashes, zero missing/dangling/unlisted/B-tree/KV/Void issues, and `Status: OK`.
- **Native durability trace:** `strace -f -yy -ttt` around the exact direct runtime integration binary separates bootstrap, hard commit, cadence, and shutdown. The hard user commit occupies trace lines 348-399 and touches only `runtime-source.aeordb`: 49 writes, one positioned header write, two data barriers, and no shadow/workspace I/O. Post-acknowledgement memory refusal then durably stores the immutable journal and restores the lease. The successful cadence occupies lines 420-504, touches no source database bytes, and services two tasks in about 25 ms through one task object, one manifest, and shadow checkpoint/selector publication. Its 13 full and seven data barriers are cadence-owned durability, not per-user-write amplification. Shutdown work is separately visible at lines 505-522 and excluded from both measurements.
- **Boundary:** the installed v4 runtime satisfies the P6 commit-latency contract: user acknowledgement excludes parser, posting/NVT, task, manifest, checkpoint, and selector work. The existing v3 query-compatible metadata writer remains synchronous by deliberate transitional design; P7 must remove it only when every reader cuts over to v4 authority. No claim that all indexing is asynchronous is valid before that coordinated cutover.
- **Evidence:** machine-readable results and retained artifact hashes are in [`p6-4c-producer-commit-and-resource.json`](../evidence/p6-4c-producer-commit-and-resource.json). The disposable database and logs remain under `/home/wyatt/.cache/codex/aeordb-tests/real-world/`; no production database, deployment, or destructive operation was used.

## P7-1 Entry Territory and Split

- **Existing authority:** Child 03 already provides the storage-neutral `ReadViewResolverV1`, exact selected-header capture, current-then-selected authorization ordering, concealment, immutable authority closure, lifecycle observation, and request-owned root pins. Child 04 supplies pending/retired lifecycle semantics. No production `ReadViewAuthoritySourceV1` or `ReadViewAuthorizerV1` implementation exists, `AppState` owns no resolver, and no HTTP or embedded namespace read currently consumes `ResolvedReadViewV1`.
- **Route surface:** the Axum server has 95 route registrations and 124 method/path operations. Path-only classification is insufficient: `/files/{*path}`, users/groups/configuration, tasks, snapshots/forks, links, and plugins combine read and mutation methods under one registration. Namespace consumers include file/list/head, query/search, batch fetch, ZIP/media download, hash retrieval, symlink read, root-aware plugin host reads, version aliases, authorized share-file reads, pagination/APOS, embedded `DirectoryOps`/`QueryEngine`, portal/client schemas, and SSE. Multi-root version/sync operations, blob transport, mutations, and operational routes require distinct no-fallthrough proofs.
- **Security and failure direction:** current authorization must run before root existence/lifecycle/contents become observable; selected-root authorization may only restrict it. Explicit `root_hash` never falls through to HEAD, share credentials cannot select historical roots, hidden paths/roots collapse to the existing concealment response, retired roots are `410`, and corrupt/unavailable authority never becomes empty/not-found success. Hash retrieval requires selected-root reachability; raw chunk/internal retrieval remains diagnostic authority only.
- **Execution split:** P7-1a freezes a method-aware production route registry and pure selector/root-response/error model in the required `root_api_reference_spec`. P7-1b implements exact first-authority/lifecycle and permission adapters. P7-1c wires handlers and embedded equivalents through their declared contract while leaving actual v4 service activation to P8. P7-2 then owns APOS and total order; P7-3 planning; P7-4 locators/plugin range continuity; P7-5 coordinated schemas/SSE/docs.

## P7-1a Root API Reference Contract

- **Frozen surface:** one production registry classifies all 95 Axum route registrations and all 124 method/path operations as single-root namespace reads, multi-root reads, content staging, hash retrieval, mutations, or operational traffic. Each operation also declares its selector location, response root shape, authorization owner, and required read-view proof. An independent source scanner compares the registry to every actual `.route(...)` registration and HTTP method constructor, so additions or method changes cannot silently bypass classification.
- **Pure contract:** `root_hash`, snapshot, and version selectors are mutually exclusive, width checked against the database hash algorithm, reject the all-zero sentinel, and canonicalize exact roots as bytes. Successful JSON and streaming responses share lowercase full-hash, lifecycle-state, and expiration metadata. Stable root/pagination/position/historical/corruption codes retain concealment as one 404 `NOT_FOUND` response where current authority requires it.
- **Proof:** the named target first failed to compile because `server::root_api` did not exist. Six focused tests now cover selector success and malformed/conflicting/bounded inputs, all three readable lifecycle states, inconsistent authority metadata, ten stable error mappings plus concealment, exact registry uniqueness/invariants, high-risk route classifications, and exact source-to-registry equality. Existing read-view and HTTP route targets pass 19 and 32 tests. Rust formatting and diff hygiene pass.
- **Retained boundary:** this slice adds no `AppState` owner, authority-source adapter, authorizer, handler extraction, selected-root storage access, service activation, persistent bytes, migration, deployment, or production database operation. P7-1b next supplies the exact native authority/lifecycle/auth adapters; P7-1c remains the sole handler-integration owner.

## P7-1b Adapter Territory and Split

- **Authority closure:** `V4FirstAuthorityPublisher` is the sole native destination owner and already has bounded immutable/system-object readers, but each public helper independently recaptures the selected header. A read-view adapter must instead bind one captured database/physical/hash/layout authority, allow monotonic HEAD/header advancement, reject replacement or regression, and decode the NamespaceRoot, namespace-tree root, semantic-state object, and immutable admission witness against the captured write high-water. It may not use the v3 `StorageEngine` or infer admission from object presence.
- **Lifecycle:** the selected root-lifecycle A/B control and immutable manifest closure exist, but there is no production point lookup. The reader must treat an absent control pair as the pre-lifecycle retained baseline, distinguish candidate/pending, logically retired, and physically reclaimed records, traverse only the fence-selected directory/page path under bounded query memory, use current configured grace without rewriting the pending record, and surface malformed/ambiguous/missing authority rather than treating it as retention.
- **Authorization:** current users, groups, API-key/share rules, revocation, and route concealment remain current authority. The existing `PermissionResolver` hardcodes current `StorageEngine` loaders. P7-1b must extract one shared ordered permission evaluator, keep the current adapter behavior byte-for-behavior compatible, and feed selected-root permission documents plus current group/key identity into a restrictive-only intersection. Root remains subject to current protected-family policy; share credentials still cannot select historical roots.
- **Execution:** P7-1b1 owns captured-header immutable authority reads; P7-1b2 owns lifecycle point lookup and grace; P7-1b3 owns shared permission evaluation and request authorizer; P7-1b4 owns combined real-file/race/resource/architecture proof. P7-1c remains the only HTTP/embedded handler integration and service-activation boundary.

## P7-1b1 Captured First-Authority Reader

- **Exact authority:** `V4FirstAuthorityPublisher::load_namespace_authority_at_captured_header` owns one `root_state` linearization across current-header validation, KV alignment, and the NamespaceRoot, namespace-tree, semantic-state, and immutable admission reads. Current HEAD/header authority may advance monotonically without changing the captured root; database, physical-instance, hash-algorithm, A/B-slot, and sequence regression are rejected. Every WholeEntity and canonical system dependency is decoded against the captured write-sequence high-water, so a valid root published later cannot leak through a stale selector.
- **Failure direction:** only an absent requested root locator returns `None`. Wrong-role roots, disappearing locators, missing semantic/tree/admission dependencies, cross-root identities, malformed bytes, sequence overflow, degraded headers, replacement, regression, and I/O/lock failures remain typed hard errors. Cancellation is checked before locking, after header/KV observations, between every dependency, and before return. The method infers no admission from object presence and has no v3 `StorageEngine`, route, service, or activation dependency.
- **Proof:** the named target first failed to compile because the captured-header reader did not exist. Five real-file tests now cover Blake3-256 and SHA-512, exact current closure, a historical root after successor publication, current-authority advance, unknown-root absence, corrupted admitted dependency, foreign physical identity, cancellation, and rejection of a post-capture root by the frozen WholeEntity high-water invariant. Adjacent read-view, first-authority, and root-migration targets pass 19, 4, and 14 tests, for 42 total passing tests. Package all-target checking passes in 1m13s with only the inherited unused test macro warning; Rust formatting, diff hygiene, and the independent 454-fixture/95-route/38-document contract gate pass.
- **Resource boundary:** one closure can include a namespace-tree entity up to the frozen 48 MiB cap. This disconnected reader remains unactivated; P7-1b4 must attach conservative request/global query reservations to the returned authority for its full `ResolvedReadView` lifetime and prove pressure refusal plus release before P7-1c can expose it.

## P6-4a Durable Journal Handoff Proof

- **Correction:** durable journal admission now publishes one existing body-free AITK-v1 `Reconcile` task keyed by the immutable journal head instead of one selected workspace task per source record. The retained in-memory continuation services at most 32 exact records from one journal load, preserves each record's original operation identity and publication sequence at the mutation coordinator, and removes the task only when the validated continuation equals the journal record count. Cancellation releases the lease without losing progress; restart deliberately resumes at zero and relies on exact mutation identities for idempotent replay.
- **Failure direction:** durable persistence happens once before bounded queue admission. Admission pressure uses that exact durable receipt rather than writing a second spill artifact. Malformed or discontinuous journals fail before task persistence; source, parser, mutation, cancellation, panic, retry, and spill failures retain or replay the exact task. Panic payload classes are preserved as bounded degradation context without increasing the reviewed suppression inventory.
- **Latency proof:** the literal 10,000-record regression completes in about 0.10 seconds in the debug test binary and observes exactly one journal persistence, one task persistence, and one pending task. A 40-record service regression proves one journal read for the first 32-record page, one for the final eight-record page, exact per-record operation/sequence observations, and completion only after the second page. The format-compatible workspace path remains the sole object/manifest/checkpoint/selector writer; P6-4c retains exact native selector/fsync tracing.
- **Focused and affected proof:** the final affected matrix passes 13 admission, 26 coordinator, 15 source, 11 recovery-store, 40 batch-publisher, 51 runtime-owner, 38 workspace-store, and 38 migration-destination tests: 232 tests with zero failures. The complete library binary passes 590 tests with zero failures. The runtime-owner architecture guards prove one bounded reconcile collection/finalization loop and preserve legacy per-record `Reconcile` compatibility.
- **Static proof:** formatting and `git diff --check` pass. The error-squelch inventory remains exactly 1,533 reviewed production occurrences and all 29 architecture tests pass after a line-only metadata refresh. Strict library Clippy reports 120 historical diagnostics (121 `error:` lines including the compiler summary) and adds no new diagnostic; its sole changed-path hit is the pre-existing `resolve_semantic_scope_work` argument-count debt. The retained log `/home/wyatt/.cache/codex/aeordb-tests/logs/p6-4a-clippy-final.log` has SHA-256 `423e3758b8fa53ba74883f5c4dc7918e84cf6f229565679c7eb67de1ef17e64e`.
- **Territory:** the completed Child 03 `NamespaceMutationCoordinator` is the sole hard-authority commit owner. Directory and version producers attach caller-specific post-commit effect fanouts; import attaches an event fanout; task queue, backup locator/import apply, server promote, CLI promote, and root wrappers use the no-op fanout. The engine currently has no engine-owned recoverable-soft subscriber. Synchronous legacy indexing still exists after selected file writes and before their caller returns; its replacement belongs to P6-2/P6-4. P5 provides disconnected typed mutation/checkpoint/artifact codecs but no runtime owner.
- **P6-1 proof shape:** add the required target compile-red first; compare a storage-neutral production state machine to an independent ordered-map model; cover in-order, duplicate, reordered, missing, overflow, restart, identity mismatch, whole-root, cancellation, and malformed boundary behavior before connecting the engine seam
- **Evidence:** the P6 entry baseline passed 104 namespace/tree/index tests. The new `coverage_runtime_spec` first failed to compile because the runtime module and ratified coverage names did not exist. The storage-neutral tracker now requires exact namespace/control authority continuity, accepts unrelated global publication-sequence gaps, treats exact duplicate delivery as idempotent, and latches every loss, reorder, stale, conflicting-duplicate, or authority-discontinuity case for reconciliation. Manifest coverage fields now use portable epoch/publication terminology without changing bytes. The focused target passed 7 tests; the affected v4 format/index matrix passed 174 tests; `cargo check -j4 --workspace --all-targets`, formatting, `git diff --check`, and the complete all-target workspace test gauntlet all passed. One initial affected-matrix command named the nonexistent `index_v1_manifest_spec` target and was corrected before tests ran.

## P6-1b Engine-Owned Soft Handoff

- **Territory and authority:** all namespace mutation producers converge on `NamespaceMutationCoordinator::execute_with_namespace`; the engine-owned offer occurs exactly once after the hard publication receipt and before the pre-existing caller-specific fanout. Directory, version, import, task, backup, server/CLI promotion, and whole-root wrappers retain their established fanout behavior. An architecture gate proves there is one coordinator call, one storage wrapper, and no producer bypass.
- **Bounded admission:** `SoftMutationHubV1` preallocates a fixed 4,096-notice queue and caps retained notice payload at 8 MiB and each notice at 256 KiB. Producer admission uses `try_lock`, checked accounting, and fallible cloning. Contention, queue exhaustion, oversize input, allocation failure, poison, malformed sequence, and an unexpected offer panic never wait for or fail the already hard-acknowledged mutation; each atomically latches reconciliation and advances bounded loss evidence.
- **Failure-direction proof:** the injected offer-panic test first failed because the committed locator remained authoritative while coverage still claimed no loss. A lock-free emergency latch now records the publication sequence and `QueueUnavailable` before the coordinator returns success. Cache/counter, engine handoff, and caller fanout unwind boundaries are independent, so failure in one cannot suppress the others.
- **TDD and focused evidence:** the initial hub/API targets failed to compile before implementation. Queue pressure, byte pressure, malformed sequence, too-small drain, deterministic contention, concurrent producer accounting, no-op/failed publication, whole-root, caller-fanout panic, oversized acknowledgement, and engine-offer panic paths are covered. The final narrow matrix passed 508 library tests, 10 coverage-runtime tests, and 33 namespace-mutation integration tests. The upload/version adjacency matrix passed 22 and 49 tests respectively.
- **Architecture and static evidence:** the exact error-suppression inventory was regenerated and reviewed at 1,457 occurrences; its gate passed all 28 tests. `coverage_runtime.rs` is classified as runtime authority rather than persistent-format conversion. `TMPDIR=/home/wyatt/.cache/codex/aeordb-tests/tmp timeout 20m cargo check -j4 --workspace --all-targets`, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Broad evidence:** `TMPDIR=/home/wyatt/.cache/codex/aeordb-tests/tmp timeout 30m cargo test -j4 --workspace --all-targets -- --test-threads=4` passed with exit code 0 across 258 passing targets and 6,046 standard passing test lines. The complete log is `/home/wyatt/.cache/codex/aeordb-tests/p6-1b-full.log`.
- **Deferred ownership:** the hub's fixed 8 MiB payload cap and preallocated slots are engine bounded but do not yet participate in `MemoryCoordinator`; the single worker, shared memory admission, draining, and latch-clear protocol belong to P6-2. Persistent journal/replay and authoritative reconciliation belong to P6-1c. No v4 writer, query activation, migration, deployment, or production operation was activated here.
- **P6-1c execution split:** (a) exact verified NamespaceRoot/SemanticState/SystemFamily authority derivation; (b) bounded postcommit reorder and frozen journal-segment encoding; (c) validated replay plus explicit diff/rebuild outcomes for absent, ambiguous, corrupt, or disconnected authority; and (d) generation-checked loss clearing with restart, cancellation, corruption, and concurrent-loss proof. Persistent publication remains a soft-worker responsibility and is not added to the hard acknowledgement path.

## P6-1c Recoverable Journal and Reconciliation

- **Exact authority:** coverage is derived from the verified NamespaceRoot, its matching SemanticState object, and the selected SystemFamily semantic projection. Missing, ambiguous, corrupt, or canceled selection remains distinguishable and never becomes an empty delta. A semantic-control change across an otherwise contiguous namespace notice window now requires authoritative reconciliation; replaying file notices alone cannot falsely claim exact coverage under new semantics.
- **Bounded ordering and encoding:** postcommit notices are bounded by count and retained bytes, reordered by publication sequence/root continuity, deduplicated exactly, and rejected to bounded diff/rebuild on conflicts, whole-root transitions, invalid identity, or pressure. Exact windows encode the frozen system mutation-journal bytes with domain-hashed UUID identities, explicit batch ordinals, frozen owner identity, and no synthetic move or whole-root record.
- **Recovery and loss clearing:** journal replay is bounded by segment count and encoded bytes, cancellation-aware, validates every frozen segment/record and chain edge, and preserves corruption evidence. The soft hub tracks every dropped notice with a monotonic loss epoch and in-flight publication guard. Loss can clear only through an opaque tracker reconciliation proof at or beyond all lost/queued evidence; stale tokens and concurrent drops cannot be erased.
- **Failure direction:** there is still no persistent sink, worker, v4 activation, parser/index work, or journal sync in the hard writer. P6-2 owns the single memory-coordinated drain/persist worker and coverage-epoch binding. A replay summary validates a chain but deliberately does not activate or apply it before that owner exists.
- **TDD and focused evidence:** the new target first failed to compile before the contracts existed. The semantic-control regression then failed by returning `Exact` and passed after the fail-closed guard. The final focused targets pass 12 coverage-journal, 10 coverage-runtime, and 28 error-squelch architecture tests. The exact suppression inventory remains 1,457 reviewed occurrences; formatting and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Broad evidence:** the first complete all-target gauntlet passed 6,057 tests with 7 ignored before the final semantic-control guard. The phase-boundary rerun after that guard passed 259 targets, 6,058 tests, and 7 ignored with zero failures. Its log is `/home/wyatt/.cache/codex/aeordb-tests/p6-1c-final-full.log`.

## P6-1d Qualification

- **Restart/failure matrix:** the coverage tracker, soft hub, journal, and namespace coordinator suites prove restart comparison, dropped/duplicate/out-of-order delivery, queue count/byte pressure, oversized acknowledgement, allocation/poison/panic failure direction, whole-root reconciliation, cancellation, malformed/corrupt authority and journal input, and concurrent loss-generation races.
- **Acknowledgement isolation:** one deterministic unit test retains the consumer queue lock while another thread offers the postcommit acknowledgement. The offer completes while the lock remains held and returns `QueueContended`, proving the producer uses nonblocking admission rather than waiting for downstream work. Existing namespace tests prove soft panic and oversize loss cannot change the already-durable hard success or suppress caller fanout. Source architecture gates retain exactly one engine-owned post-authority offer and no parser/index/journal sync in that path.
- **Bounds:** the hub preallocates fixed notice capacity, checks retained bytes before cloning, uses fallible allocations, and converts every inability to retain soft state into explicit reconciliation evidence. Journal ordering/replay is count/byte bounded and cancellation-aware. P6-2 now owns transferring these fixed bounds into the process-wide memory budget and persistent worker lifecycle.
- **Next action:** add the compile-red P6-2 coordinator target after mapping all legacy buffer, parser, task, timer, spill, checkpoint, cache, startup, and shutdown owners. The new owner must replace or adapt those paths rather than coexist as another independent index runtime.

## P6-2 Entry Territory and Split

- **Producers:** ordinary file writes currently invoke `IndexingPipeline` synchronously from two `DirectoryOps` paths; reindex owns a task-local `IndexWriteBuffer`; delete/index cleanup and manual index operations call `IndexManager`; all ultimately mutate the engine-wide `SharedIndexWriteBuffer`. The P6-1 engine handoff is the sole post-authority namespace notice producer and is not yet consumed by a worker.
- **Consumers:** query/search load through `IndexManager`; the server owns a separate periodic `flush_index_buffer_if_due` timer; task reindex polls its wrapper's count/time state; shutdown directly forces the shared buffer; emergency spill snapshots it; runtime metrics and the memory dashboard read its stats; configuration activation stages clean/dirty/publication/count/time limits.
- **Existing strengths to preserve:** the legacy shared buffer already applies the ratified 262,144-mutation/30-second defaults, process-wide `MemoryCoordinator` reservations, byte-bounded publication batches, clean LRU/TTL eviction, failed-flush restoration, shutdown flush, and emergency-spill capture. P6-2 must adapt these proven behaviors and tests, not discard them.
- **Architectural gap:** ownership remains split among `DirectoryOps`, `IndexingPipeline`, `IndexManager`, `IndexWriteBuffer`, `task_worker`, the server timer, shutdown, and emergency spill. Large `FieldIndex.entries`/`values` maps are still the mutable unit. A v1 coordinator added beside those paths would violate the single-owner contract.
- **Execution split:** P6-2a freezes the storage-neutral active/frozen memtable and memory state machine; P6-2b binds journals/checkpoints/recovery; P6-2c converges parser and all index mutation producers; P6-2d hands timer, pressure, cache, startup, shutdown, metrics, and spill to the same owner. P6-3 alone activates immutable page/pointer publication and query coverage.
- **P6-2a falsifier:** under a tiny process/local budget, admission must reserve before cloning, exact duplicate delivery must be idempotent, conflicting/stale identities must fail without mutation, active work may continue beside one frozen batch, failed completion must retain or explicitly spill every dirty byte, cancellation must not consume state, and shutdown must reject new work until the final batch is resolved.

## P6-2a Bounded Coordinator State Machine

- **TDD:** the dedicated `index_coordinator_spec` first failed to compile because no v4 coordinator module or contract existed. A tight exact-fit memory test then failed because provisional order-key headroom exceeded the true retained footprint; ordered records now expose a nonallocating canonical key-length check, and admission reserves the exact modeled bytes before materializing the key or cloning the record. A final abandoned-publication test failed on the absent retry contract before attempt-qualified frozen-batch reissue was added.
- **Single bounded state:** one storage-neutral coordinator owns a deterministically ordered active memtable and at most one frozen memtable. It validates hash-width identity, role, operation identity, publication sequence, canonical record framing, and monotonic time before changing state. Exact duplicates are no-ops; stale or same-sequence conflicting mutations fail closed; newer mutations replace by canonical index/role/order key.
- **Memory and batching:** every retained mutation owns an exact `IndexDirtyBuffers` reservation, local mutation bytes are checked across active plus frozen state, and publication snapshots use bounded critical durability reservations. Flush triggers cover mutation count, age, process pressure, explicit request, and shutdown. Deterministic prefix batches never exceed `publication_batch_max_bytes`; active work may continue while one batch is frozen.
- **Failure direction:** cancellation before freeze changes nothing. Failed completion restores every distinct frozen record and merges observation counts while newer active state wins. Foreign tokens cannot consume another coordinator's state. Abandoned handles can be reissued with a new attempt identity; stale attempts are refused while the exact frozen batch remains recoverable. Shutdown rejects new admissions, requires all active/frozen state to resolve, then enters `Stopped`.
- **Focused proof:** 13 coordinator tests cover invalid options/identity/bytes, malformed records, exact-fit global and local pressure, exact accounting, duplicate/replacement/stale/conflict semantics, count/age/process-pressure triggers, bounded partial batches, active-plus-frozen operation, failure restore, pre-freeze cancellation, foreign and stale attempts, retry, clock rollback, and draining. Both contiguous and posting nonallocating order-key lengths match materialized keys.
- **Affected/static proof:** 32 copy-on-write, 46 frozen index-reference, 18 memory-coordinator, and 28 error-squelch architecture tests pass. The exact reviewed suppression inventory remains 1,457 because suppression-shaped constructs found during review were removed rather than allowlisted. Workspace all-target checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass; the only check warning is the pre-existing unused `require_wasm_parser` test macro.

P6-2b owns durable journal/checkpoint recovery; P6-2c replaces all producer paths; P6-2d transfers runtime lifecycle ownership. P6-3 remains the sole immutable index publication/query activation phase.

## P6-2d Durable Scope-Ordinal Authority Entry

- **Ratification:** the owner ratified the recommended selected-shadow ordinal decisions on 2026-08-13. The authority must persist every operation claim before returning, bind retries to the exact transition, preserve nonreuse through a scope-local checked high-water, and serialize concurrent allocation through selector-last compare-and-swap. No in-memory production allocation map is permitted.
- **Landing split:** P6-2d-a freezes the storage-neutral selected-state/CAS contract and proves allocation, preservation, exact retry, operation-ID conflict, concurrent selector conflict, cancellation, stale authority, and store-failure behavior. P6-2d-b binds it to immutable checkpoint bytes, selected scope manifests, ordinal/reverse directory reads, and a concrete synced storage adapter. Only that complete binding closes the durable-authority TODO.
- **Entry commit:** `8884730`; branch `development`; legacy v3 indexing and every service/runtime route remain authoritative and unchanged.

## P6-2d-c2a Native Immutable IndexArtifact Authority

- **Implementation:** `V4FirstAuthorityPublisher` now exposes one native v4 immutable IndexArtifact boundary: an allocation-free exact-length probe, a bounded exact loader, and an idempotent batch publisher. Publication accepts at most 4,097 artifacts and 64 MiB of encoded values, rejects duplicate/fabricated identities and wrong KV roles, and treats an all-existing batch as exact idempotence without consuming a header sequence. A mixed retry consumes one write sequence and one entry count only for each missing entity.

## P6-2d-c2c Native Recovery Store and Bounded Registry

## P6-2d-c3a Engine Installation Boundary

- **Single ownership:** `StorageEngine` now retains its existing soft-mutation hub under one shared `Arc` and admits at most one `IndexRuntimeOwnerV1` through a namespace-authority-serialized installation boundary. Before installation, existing hub behavior is unchanged. After installation, post-authority acknowledgement and forced-loss signals enter that exact owner and health/metrics read its lock-free cached snapshot; migration capture remains a separate optional subscriber rather than becoming a second runtime owner.
- **Nonblocking failure direction:** owner acknowledgement refresh uses a nonblocking soft-hub observation. If the queue lock is contended, a hard-acknowledged mutation still returns immediately and latches/project its typed reconciliation evidence without waiting on the worker mutex or observability lock. A mismatched owner configuration and duplicate installation fail without replacing or disconnecting the engine hub.
- **Poison versus pressure:** a nonblocking soft-hub snapshot now distinguishes ordinary queue contention from poison. Contention may retain the previous cached metric, while poison forces `QueueUnavailable` reconciliation and updates the cached owner evidence. This avoids converting a damaged queue into an apparently accepted postcommit mutation merely because the failure was observed through the metrics refresh path.
- **Physical reopen preparation:** `V4FirstAuthorityPublisher::open` is now the one production no-follow reopen path for an existing v4 shadow file. It validates the selected header/hot-tail authority, reconstructs the exact declared KV layout and one durability coordinator, and reuses the normal publisher constructor for final KV/header agreement. Recovery and migration tests no longer maintain duplicated reopen sequences.
- **TDD and focused proof:** the engine-installation target first failed to compile on the absent installation and observation contract; the contention distinction then failed on the absent typed error. Three internal tests prove inactive observation, mismatched-capacity refusal, one-time installation, exact shared-hub visibility, forced-loss propagation, poison, and a postcommit offer completing while the queue lock is deliberately held. The focused matrix passes 3 engine, 21 runtime-owner, 33 namespace-mutation, 10 coverage-runtime, 7 migration-capture-runtime, and 28 error-squelch architecture tests with one job and one test thread. Workspace/all-target checking, formatting, and diff hygiene pass with only the historical unused `require_wasm_parser` macro warning. The reviewed suppression inventory grows by exactly one typed authority-error conversion to 1,509 entries; two optional-observability suppressions found during review were removed instead of allowlisted.
- **Activation boundary and next action:** this unit installs only a recovering, disconnected shadow owner; no constructor activates it, no production task enters it, and v3 queries and writers remain authoritative. A provisional content-only wrapper was deliberately removed because it could pair an arbitrary v3 engine with an unrelated shadow destination. P6-2d-c3b must bind the owner to explicit source/shadow identity, derive content-only versus complete state from selected semantic authority, recover every explicitly described selected checkpoint under count/byte/cancellation bounds, and resolve any missing, corrupt, or discontinuous state as typed degradation rather than leaving an installed owner indefinitely `Recovering`.

## P6-2d-c3b Native Recovery Installation

- **Source/shadow identity:** `IndexRuntimeShadowIdentityV1` is derived from the admitted migration preflight or owner and binds logical database, migration, source and destination physical instances, native source-file identity, hash profile, and SystemFamily fingerprint. Installation re-observes the open source file and selected non-degraded v4 destination before retaining either authority; mismatches and cancellation do not consume the one installation slot.
- **Bounded selected authority:** `load_selected_semantic_authority` reads the selected NamespaceRoot, SemanticState, and root-admission witness without loading the namespace tree. Runtime recovery accepts an explicit bounded descriptor set, retains shared-memory admission for its ordering workspace until recovery ends, validates every descriptor against the selected authority, and never infers missing checkpoints from semantic definition counts. Content-only and complete zero-checkpoint states start a clean build; explicitly selected missing, corrupt, discontinuous, or wrong-semantic checkpoints install one typed degraded owner rather than remaining `Recovering`.
- **Canonical semantic publication:** immutable semantic objects publish through the shared native WholeEntity/KV/header authority as canonical system `FileRecord` plus one system chunk. Batch count, kind-specific byte caps, canonical identity, timestamp, allocation, duplicate, collision, database, and role checks occur before authority selection; retry is idempotent and successor selection continues through the existing first-authority path.
- **One runtime boundary:** `StorageEngine` serializes preparation, refreshes the recovered owner under hard namespace authority, and installs one `NativeIndexRuntimeV1` in a `OnceLock`. The refresh closes the acknowledgement window between recovery and installation: queued work is observed and any pre-install soft loss changes the installed state to `Degraded`. Legacy v3 query authority remains unchanged.
- **Adversarial corrections:** review caught and fixed six false-success risks before landing: a pre-install queue/loss race, treating a nonzero semantic definition count as proof that a selected checkpoint must exist, releasing descriptor-order memory accounting while the vector remained live, accepting cancellation after recovery but before installation, installing against semantic authority that changed during recovery, and making canonical semantic-object idempotence accidentally depend on a later caller's publication timestamp. After checkpoint I/O, the installer acquires the publisher's existing root-authority mutex, reloads and revalidates destination identity plus the exact selected root, semantic-state object, and root publication sequence, and retains that guard through owner installation; successor root publication therefore cannot cross the installation linearization. Cancellation is checked both at this final frontier and immediately before install. Existing semantic objects are now loaded and exactly content-verified under that same authority boundary, with their original canonical `FileRecord` timestamps reused by later ensure-present calls. The broad architecture pass identified both new physical-authority ownership edges; their allowlists admit only the installer and assert that it forwards the shared retirement owner, cannot construct an independent journal owner, has no service/timer activation token, and cannot publish root authority directly. A final falsifying test proved one poisoned-queue acknowledgement was counted twice; the owner now forces a second loss only when the acknowledgement itself succeeded and observability independently fails.
- **Final proof:** the corrected tree passes the complete workspace/all-target suite serially: 292 result groups, 6,508 passed, 0 failed, and 7 pre-existing opt-in crash-injection tests ignored. ## P6-2d-c3c Entry Map and Proof Split

- **Entry commit and baseline:** c3c enters at pushed commit `2189129` (`Implement native v4 index runtime recovery installation`). The focused owner/recovery/store/task baseline passes 57 tests serially with one Cargo job and one test thread.
- **Producer/consumer map:** `IndexProducerCoordinatorV1` is the only task-retention owner and calls `IndexProducerSpillStoreV1` on admission pressure or retry exhaustion. `IndexCoordinatorV1` is the only mutation-memtable owner and freezes one deterministically ordered `FrozenIndexBatchV1`; `IndexRuntimeOwnerV1::flush` is the only consumer of `IndexRuntimeBatchPublisherV1` and drops that exact batch only after a matching successor-checkpoint receipt. Native immutable artifacts and A/B `IndexOperationControlV1` selection already converge through `NativeIndexRecoveryStoreV1` and `publish_index_recovery_checkpoint_v1`. `IndexTaskCheckpointV1` already carries one bounded external-workspace descriptor, and SystemFamily kind 4 already classifies the protected index workspace. No concrete index-workspace reader/writer, batch publisher, or producer spill store exists.
- **Gaps exposed by concrete publication:** mock publishers hid two false-success paths. Frozen mutations intentionally contain ordered derived records but no complete source-root/journal closure, so a persisted dirty overlay must not claim complete journal coverage. Generic checkpoint recovery validates immutable attachments and journals but currently does not open or validate an external descriptor. Also, a publication batch can exceed the checkpoint resume-key cap, so serializing all records into `resume_key` is forbidden; the external workspace is the frozen large-state boundary.
- **Execution split:** c3c-a freezes independent index-workspace manifest/object bytes before a writer; c3c-b adds private immutable append/reopen; c3c-c binds a cumulative workspace head to the existing selector-last checkpoint authority; c3c-d makes external dirty-overlay recovery bounded and truthful; c3c-e implements body-free producer spill through the same owner; c3c-f runs crash, tamper, bounds, restart, and architecture proof. P6-3 still exclusively owns page/manifest/active-pointer publication and query-visible coverage.
- **Failure direction:** an object or manifest durably written before checkpoint selection is an unselected recoverable/orphan artifact, never active index authority. A runtime frozen batch remains retained for every definitely-unselected failure. Once the exact successor checkpoint is selected, uncertainty is commit-unknown and restart must select either the prior validated head or the exact complete successor. Missing, truncated, tampered, foreign, over-budget, or unopenable selected external state degrades/reconciles; it can never become an empty successful batch.

## P6-2d-c3c-a Frozen Workspace Contract

- **Persistent contract:** independently generated AIWM v1 manifests are exact 208-byte CRC-protected cumulative heads; AIWO v1 immutable objects carry database/destination/workspace/runtime/object identity, database hash profile, publication bounds, payload BLAKE3, and whole-object CRC under a 512 MiB cap. AIRB v1 stores retry-stable, strict-canonical frozen mutations without attempt identity. AITK v1 stores only task identity, roots, journal-or-canonical-scope closure, and no file body. Both database hash-width profiles have independent manifest, runtime-batch, and producer-task fixtures.

All 28 error-squelch architecture tests pass without allowlist growth after removing eleven suppression-shaped conversions/defaults/panics.

## P6-2d-c3c-b Private Workspace Store Entry

- **Entry and scope:** c3c-b enters at pushed green commit `45df6f0`. It owns one disconnected private immutable workspace store and the minimum streaming codec support required by that store. Future c3c-c runtime publication, c3c-d selected recovery, and c3c-e producer spill are consumers; no create/open, service, timer, query, v3 writer, or selected-checkpoint caller may activate it in this landing unit.
- **Identity and byte contract:** creation requires explicit nonzero database, destination physical-instance, workspace, and runtime identities plus the database hash profile. The selected head later exposes workspace ID, manifest BLAKE3, durable sequence, and durable bytes. `durable_bytes` is exactly the manifest's cumulative immutable-object bytes; the workspace disk ceiling separately includes every immutable object, every 208-byte manifest, and the pending artifact currently being admitted.
- **Crash direction:** one object is synced and installed no-clobber before its successor manifest is synced and installed no-clobber. A crash before manifest installation leaves an unselected orphan object that an exact retry may reuse; a conflicting existing object or manifest fails closed. Append advances the in-memory head only after the new object, successor manifest, and exact predecessor digest validate; selected reopen and resume validate the complete predecessor/object closure under hard traversal bounds.
- **Bounded reopen:** selected reopen derives canonical sequence/object paths, traverses predecessor manifests in reverse under explicit count/byte limits, rejects gaps/substitution/foreign identity/cumulative disagreement, and streams large object validation. Runtime frames reserve only the current bounded frame plus the prior canonical comparison key under `IndexDirtyBuffers`; producer-task payloads use their frozen small cap. Cancellation and memory/capacity refusal release every reservation and never synthesize an empty success.
- **Central falsifiers:** a valid standalone manifest with the wrong predecessor/object/cumulative closure must never reopen, and a large AIRB object must append/reopen without allocating a second payload-sized buffer. The dedicated target is `index_runtime_workspace_store_spec`; it must compile-red before the first writer exists.
- **Review correction:** native no-clobber installation can report uncertain completion after the final hard-link exists but before its pending name is removed or parent state is fully synced. Exact-target recovery therefore also removes the pending name, syncs its parent, and preserves either a typed cleanup failure or both uncertainty causes if that durable cleanup cannot be completed; c3c-f retains the injected post-install crash/error matrix.
- **Append complexity correction:** a full object-directory inventory before every immutable append would make a long-lived workspace quadratic. The writer instead carries a three-state append invariant (`clean`, exact unselected object, or inventory reconciliation required). Normal successful appends remain O(1); reopen and failed/uncertain object installation trigger one bounded inventory reconciliation before the next append.
- **Retry capacity correction:** cumulative/disk-ceiling accounting still counts an exact unselected orphan object once, but free-space admission for its retry requests only the new manifest bytes. Recovery therefore never demands enough free space to rewrite an immutable object that it validates and reuses in place.
- **Next-unit portability dependency:** the frozen external-workspace descriptor currently validates its filesystem path with the AeorDB namespace-path validator. c3c-c must replace that semantic check with canonical native-path validation before selector publication, otherwise a valid Windows drive path would be rejected even though the c3c-b store itself accepts native absolute paths.
- **Completed implementation:** `DurableIndexRuntimeWorkspaceV1` creates or resumes one private workspace, streams AIRB payloads through a two-pass plan/emitter, installs each immutable AIWO object before its cumulative AIWM manifest, and advances its in-process head only after exact byte, digest, predecessor, count, and byte closure validation. Normal successful append is O(1); only restart or uncertain/failed object installation invokes one bounded canonical inventory reconciliation. Selected reopen walks the complete predecessor chain under explicit count/byte limits, streams object validation with per-frame shared-memory admission, and rejects every path, identity, sequence, kind, digest, cumulative, semantic, and amplification disagreement. The store remains disconnected from startup, services, timers, queries, v3 authority, and production databases.
- **Crash and retry proof:** exact object-before-manifest prefixes are reusable only by byte-identical retries. Conflicting names fail closed; uncertain no-clobber completion reopens the exact target, removes the pending name, syncs its parent, and preserves both cleanup/uncertainty causes when durable cleanup cannot be proven. An already-installed exact orphan counts once against the workspace ceiling but requests only successor-manifest free space. Zero identities, noncanonical native paths/names, symlink substitutions, cancellation, memory refusal, capacity refusal, truncation, repaired-integrity corruption, and foreign authority never synthesize a selected head.
- **Focused and affected evidence:** the dedicated native-filesystem target passes 19/19 cases, and a source unit proof covers exact-orphan incremental allocation. The affected v4 matrix passes 162 tests; independent format and reader hardening pass 76 and 2 tests; the contract gate verifies 448 independent cases, 95 routes, and 38 documentation pages; all-target workspace checking and Rust formatting pass. Focused Clippy reports no finding in the changed workspace modules and remains blocked only by the pre-existing `range_extract.rs` never-loop lint.
- **Broad and real-filesystem evidence:** the final exact-source workspace/all-target gate passes 294 result groups, 6,539 tests, zero failures, and seven intentional ignores. Retained log `/home/wyatt/.cache/codex/aeordb-tests/p6-2d-c3c-b-full-r2.log` has SHA-256 `8a8d6588fb782150ddb94dc0f44eabd6ee13a41b55dc287751669c4d7ea6da4a`. The dedicated integration target exercises real create, sync, append, crash-prefix, reopen, tamper, permissions, and no-follow behavior in disposable native filesystem directories; no live-server route exists yet because activation is deliberately deferred.

## P6-2d-c3c-c Selector-Last Runtime Publication Entry

- **Entry and boundary:** c3c-c enters at pushed green commit `a1843fd`. It composes the disconnected workspace with the existing immutable IndexArtifact and IndexOperationControl authority; no startup, timer, service, route, query, v3 writer, or production database may call the new publisher in this landing unit.
- **Invariant correction:** the current runtime restores a definitely unselected frozen batch into the active memtable, so its next flush receives a new batch identity. That behavior contradicts exact workspace retry and can strand an unselected durable prefix. Retryable and canceled preselection outcomes must instead retain the exact frozen batch internally and issue only a fresh attempt handle.
- **Publication contract:** one retry-stable object identity and timestamp feeds one cumulative workspace head. A no-journal dirty-overlay checkpoint names that exact head through its external descriptor, keeps journal watermarks at zero, and makes no complete-coverage claim. The checkpoint artifact is durable before selector publication. Any selector error is resolved by reopening authority: the prior root is definitely unselected, the exact successor is committed, and every missing/foreign/unopenable observation is commit-unknown and fails closed.
- **Portability prerequisite:** external descriptor bytes represent a canonical native filesystem path, not an AeorDB namespace path. The shared persisted-path validator must accept canonical POSIX and Windows drive forms independent of the build host while rejecting relative, backslash, dot-segment, root-only, empty-segment, NUL, and trailing-separator forms.
- **Implementation and adversarial correction:** `DurableIndexRuntimeBatchPublisherV1` now composes the cumulative private workspace with the existing immutable-checkpoint store and sole `IndexOperationControl` selector. Construction binds exact database, destination physical instance, hash profile, owner, source root, and fresh unselected workspace authority. Runtime retries retain one frozen batch while issuing fresh attempt handles. Review found that a durable object followed by a failed manifest install could lose its retry-stable creation time before a checkpoint existed; a compile-green/crash-prefix regression first reproduced the resulting self-collision after clock advance. The publisher now retains object identity and timestamp before entering workspace I/O and clears them only after exact selector completion.
- **Failure and portability proof:** eight focused publisher tests and 21 owner tests prove cumulative two-batch heads, object-before-manifest retry, selector refusal, bounded amplified errors, exact committed-but-error reopen, unreadable/missing committed-state uncertainty, real native A/B authority, stable batch identity, fresh attempt identity, and no live activation. The 199-test affected v4 matrix and all 28 suppression-architecture tests pass; the exact 1,510-entry reviewed inventory receives only 18 line-location refreshes and no new suppression.
- **Static and broad proof:** the frozen contract gate passes 448 independent fixtures, 95 routes, and 38 documents. Workspace/all-target checking, formatting, and diff hygiene pass with only the historical unused `require_wasm_parser` test macro warning. Strict Clippy retains 123 historical findings, including the pre-existing denied `range_extract.rs` never-loop, and reports none in a c3c-c file; retained log `/home/wyatt/.cache/codex/aeordb-tests/p6-2d-c3c-c-clippy-r2.log` has SHA-256 `9d05c56d985c4eb559be36932d2c7d62ff30a92f4362da5a0d5e84da02b4f744`. ## P6-2d-c3c-e Durable Producer Spill Entry

- **Entry:** c3c-e enters at pushed green commit `0070a0f`. `IndexProducerCoordinatorV1` remains the sole task-retention authority and may release work only after an exact durable spill receipt; the disconnected cumulative workspace publisher remains the sole selector owner.

The frozen AITK codec already stores operation/kind/publication/root/journal-or-scope identity without file bodies, and workspace reopen already validates producer objects, but no producer append writer or concrete spill store exists. Runtime append and selector pending state are currently batch-specific and must be generalized rather than copied.
- **Central falsifier:** a selector, workspace, durability, cancellation, malformed-receipt, or retry failure must leave the exact producer task retained, while an acknowledged spill must be reopenable by its operation identity and selected checkpoint key without writing file content. Exact duplicate delivery before or after restart must return the same durable identity instead of appending another object.
- **Boundary:** c3c-e changes no frozen bytes and activates no startup, service, timer, HTTP, query, v3 writer, or production database. Replay scheduling and live drain remain in c3d-c3f; this unit must leave persisted tasks deterministically discoverable and independently validated for that owner.
- **Implemented authority:** runtime batches and body-free producer tasks now pass through one kind-parameterized object-before-manifest append state machine, one cumulative AIWM chain, one immutable dirty-overlay checkpoint, and the existing `IndexOperationControl` selector. The producer object ID is retry-stable over runtime plus operation identity; AITK allocation is admitted under `IndexDirtyBuffers` before allocation and retained through durable append/readback. Prepared and pending state is identity-typed so a runtime batch cannot overwrite a producer retry or vice versa.
- **Release and failure direction:** `IndexProducerCoordinatorV1` now rejects a spill receipt unless its spill ID is the exact producer operation ID and its artifact key has the database hash width. Selector refusal, cancellation, malformed identity, workspace/resource failure, and commit uncertainty retain recoverable work. Exact selected duplicates revalidate the immutable producer bytes and return the selected checkpoint without appending a second object; mixed runtime/producer successors preserve truthful cumulative kind counts and sequence.

The independent contract gate passes 448 fixtures, 95 routes, and 38 documents.
- **Broad and static proof:** the exact-source workspace/all-target gauntlet passes 295 result groups, 6,564 tests, zero failures, and seven intentional ignores. ## P6-2d-c3d Single Cadence Entry

- **Entry and retained blocker:** c3d enters at pushed green commit `d3d21b2`. C3c source, Linux broad proof, native Darwin proof, and Windows GNU compile/link proof are landed; exact native Windows execution remains open because the configured `win11vm` proxy endpoint refuses connections. That infrastructure gap remains explicit and does not authorize inferring native behavior.
- **Territory:** the one cadence consumes the installed runtime owner's active or exact frozen mutations, the shared memory coordinator's pressure state, configured mutation-count and age thresholds, the runtime clock and cancellation token, and one explicitly constructed selector-last durable publisher. The existing five-second index timer is its only scheduler. Legacy buffered indexes remain a separate compatibility authority until cutover, but the same timer tick must attempt legacy and v4 due work independently so either failure cannot starve the other. Cached observability consumes owner transitions; c3e later owns draining/final shutdown publication, and c3f later owns fresh/resumed publisher construction and production create/open activation.
- **Persistent-identity boundary:** one frozen runtime batch may contain records for several index IDs, so the cumulative dirty overlay cannot be assigned to an arbitrary per-index recovery descriptor. The workspace already binds the global `coordinator_id`; c3d therefore accepts an explicitly constructed durable publisher and does not invent its persistent operation identity. C3f must construct or resume that publisher from the dedicated runtime-overlay descriptor and selected workspace evidence.
- **Central falsifiers:** count, age, and shared process-memory pressure must all enter the same owner flush path with no requested-reason override; cancellation and retryable preselection failure must retain exact work; concurrent service calls must serialize one mutable publisher; an absent cadence must not affect legacy work; and source architecture must prove no second interval, task, worker, selector, synchronous postcommit flush, or v3 query-authority change.
- **Implementation:** `IndexRuntimeCadenceV1` serializes one selector-last publisher and delegates every due decision to the installed runtime owner. The existing five-second legacy index timer calls both legacy and v4 due paths on every tick and observes their typed outcomes independently. Fatal zero-clock or poisoned-publisher failures latch bounded degraded evidence while retaining queued work; expected cancellation is debug-level control flow. Installation stores exactly one cadence only after runtime ID, logical database ID, destination physical-instance ID, hash algorithm, and nonzero clock all agree with the installed runtime authorities.
- **Adversarial correction:** final review caught that matching only the runtime ID would allow an in-process caller to pair a publisher from another database or migration destination with this engine. The installation boundary now compares all four independent authority dimensions before constructing or consuming the `OnceLock`. A real file-backed regression independently varies runtime, database, destination, and SHA-512 hash authority, then proves every refusal leaves the slot available for the exact publisher.
- **Linux proof:** the final affected matrix passes 566 library, 29 suppression-architecture, 13 coordinator, 25 publisher, 24 owner, 18 memory, and 12 migration-destination tests. ## P6-2c-d Producer Convergence Entry

- **Territory and compatibility boundary:** ordinary namespace changes produce exact mutation journals; delete cleanup, configuration retirement, reindex, repair, explicit legacy mutation, legacy migration, definition build, and compaction produce root-pinned maintenance intents. Delete cleanup and reindex remain distinct identities despite both mapping to scoped rebuild work.
- **Engine seam and lock order:** an engine without an installed runtime returns `None` and preserves byte-for-byte legacy behavior. An installed runtime acquires source hard authority first, captures the current source HEAD and hard frontier, reloads and validates current destination semantic authority, then admits through the cadence. Callers cannot submit journal kinds or stale installation-time semantics through this facade.

## P6-2c-d4 Durable Soft-Mutation Journal Handoff

- **Rollback-safe source lease:** the shared soft hub now leases one bounded notice/byte/record prefix while retaining its queue capacity and telemetry accounting. New acknowledgements may append behind the lease. A failed or cancelled handoff restores the exact prefix ahead of those appends; only a successful immutable-journal barrier plus durable task admission commits the lease. A lock-free pending signal remains true for queued or leased work and is cleared only while the queue lock proves both are empty, so stale cached observability cannot starve the timer.
- **Authority and latency boundary:** the existing timer uses the nonblocking source hard-authority guard. Under that short guard it captures only HEAD, hard-publication frontier, and the exact queue lease; contention defers soft capture without blocking a writer. Destination semantic-state loading, shared-memory reservation, cloning, ordering, encoding, immutable publication, task publication, and `fsync` occur after source authority is released. The first publication sequence may internally derive a zero predecessor boundary without weakening the public nonzero `CoverageBoundaryV1` constructor.
- **Durability and restart direction:** encoded journal bytes are fully decoded and their derived identity is checked before immutable storage. The journal is synchronized before any producer task may reference it. Every exact record is then persisted through the cadence-owned selected workspace before it can join the in-memory coordinator; pressure spill and duplicate replay use that same publisher. Any journal, task, cancellation, queue, poison, or memory failure leaves the source lease recoverable. Cancellation after immutable sync retains an unselected immutable artifact and admits no task.
- **Memory and amplification bounds:** the working reservation conservatively covers one cloned retained window, two maximum artifact buffers, mutation IDs, 10,000 borrowed record descriptors, the largest notice, and fixed overhead while the original queue reservation remains charged. Lease capacity is counted separately from queue slots. The current durable task handoff publishes each exact record through the selected workspace; this is restart-safe but can require O(record count) selector/barrier work. P6-4 explicitly owns measurement at the 10,000-record bound and a format-compatible batching correction if the latency/I/O envelope is missed.
- **Adversarial corrections:** review removed a cached-telemetry scheduling dependency, a second queue-lock snapshot, blocking source-authority acquisition, destination semantic-state I/O under source authority, duplicated journal-admission owner logic, and two newly introduced error-suppression shapes. Poisoned rollback or lease-state mismatch now emits explicit evidence and latches coverage loss rather than silently continuing.
- **Proof:** six internal coverage-runtime tests, nine cadence tests, three owner-internal tests, and the 124-test affected matrix pass, covering exact rollback ordering, concurrent append/capacity, complete/partial leases, initial sequence one, stale telemetry, journal-before-task order, malformed and forged journal identity, journal/task durability refusal, cancellation before task admission, post-sync cancellation, shared-memory refusal with exact lease restoration/reservation release, duplicates, pressure, restart, and real file-backed source/shadow runtime servicing. ## P6-2c-d5a Bounded Maintenance Scan Contract

- **Entry and territory:** d5a enters at pushed green commit `20e1aa9`. - **Bounded scan contract:** every page binds an exact immutable namespace root, canonical scope, exclusive canonical resume path, nonzero document/retained-byte/path-byte limits, strict path order, complete revision hashes, and exact completion/continuation closure. A page retains a `MemoryOwner::Task` reservation covering its retained allocation capacities until consumption or drop. Cancellation, malformed roots/paths, nonadvancing or empty-incomplete pages, count/byte overflow, capacity under-accounting, and wrong-owner or undersized reservations fail closed with request and returned-page failures kept distinct.
- **Restart and identity direction:** the cursor is deliberately in-memory and absent from the frozen AITK producer payload. Restart replays the retained task from its scope rather than trusting an unselected cursor. A deterministic per-document operation ID binds parent operation, task kind, root, revision, and path, so ordinal claims and ordered mutations remain idempotent and distinct across replay without sharing one parent operation key across every file. Its Blake3-256 golden vector is pinned as `1ed70d379756b5eb7071e53cf4969938`.

The affected admission, coordinator, worker, and workspace targets pass 58 additional tests, preserving all existing journal/task/persistence behavior. All 29 suppression-architecture tests, workspace all-target checking, Rust formatting, diff hygiene, and the independent 448-fixture/95-route/38-document contract gate pass; only the inherited unused test-macro warning remains. The production adapter, owner lock split, selected-task replay, and installed-cadence service remain the explicit d5b-e work; d5a changes no frozen bytes, route, query authority, timer, startup path, or production database.

## P6-2c-d5b1 Exact Historical Revision Adapter

- **Production boundary:** one native source now resolves a file only through the exact immutable namespace root carried by producer evidence. ## P6-2c-d5b2 Seekable Historical Maintenance Scanner

- **Production boundary:** `NativeIndexMaintenanceScanSourceV1` now pages an exact immutable namespace root and canonical scope through the frozen d5a contract. ## P6-2c-d5b4 Native Parser Executor Adapter

- **Frozen authority and compatibility:** `NativeIndexParserExecutorV1` consumes only the exact namespace root, FileRecord revision, parser plan, dependency table, document-input limit, and cancellation authority supplied by the worker. It validates the three built-in dependency identities, versions, roles, profiles, and semantic fingerprints; never consults the mutable parser registry, `parsers.json`, plugin manager, `IndexingPipeline`, current HEAD, or path keys. Corrected-v1 MIME handling normalizes a valid essence, rejects wildcard and malformed media types, recognizes `application/json` and valid `application/*+json`, applies registry-before-raw-JSON-before-native order, rejects duplicate JSON keys, and treats no native match as ordinary absence. Explicit and registry WASM candidates fail with typed dependency-unavailable evidence until the corrected archive/executor exists.
- **Exact body and failure direction:** document bytes are reconstructed only from the admitted FileRecord's ordered chunk hashes through bounded verified historical chunk reads. Every chunk width, accumulated size, declared total size, and available whole-content hash is checked before parsing. Cancellation is checked before admission, between chunk reads, and around native execution. Input-policy refusal and parser-structure/malformed failures become deterministic unindexable evidence; cancellation, dependency absence, storage/integrity failures, and memory refusal remain typed operational failures. Parser `NotApplicable` maps to collector-level missing semantics instead of degrading an index.
- **Memory boundary:** body capacity is charged to `MemoryOwner::StreamingRead` and parser workspace/output to `MemoryOwner::ParserPlugin`, both under maintenance admission and released on every success, cancellation, corruption, and dependency-failure path. The collector retains its policy-derived parser-output reservation through selection and collection. This proves bounded admission, but does not claim allocator-level peak proof for inherited native parsers: compressed Office/ODF expansion and adversarial high-node JSON are now explicit P6-4 resource falsifiers that must be measured and bounded before producer activation.

## P6-2c-d5e Installed Bounded Producer Service Entry

Installed native defaults bound file/directory entities, B-tree/path traversal, documents/page, retained page bytes, semantic definitions, ordinal claims, parser input, and one task/page per service attempt. The cooperative wall budget is checked between attempts; each synchronous attempt remains independently count/input/memory bounded and cancellation-aware rather than being unsafely preempted mid-source or parser call.

## P6-3c1 First-Authority Active Pointer Entry

- **One physical authority:** `V4FirstAuthorityPublisher` now owns the stable A/B active-pointer entities for ScopeCatalog, FieldIndex, and FieldNvt. Pointer keys retain their frozen bytes through one shared key derivation helper. Publication validates exact database/profile/owner identity, the deterministic inactive slot and sequence, a complete durable target-manifest closure, canonical WholeEntity representation, and current KV/header alignment before the existing stable-entity dependency/header/KV authority may select it.
- **Closure and retry direction:** selection reuses the P6-3a planner over both structurally valid slots and validates every immediate directory root; FieldIndex additionally closes through its exact ValueStore and ScopeCatalog manifests. Structurally valid but closure-invalid slots remain visible for deterministic rewrite planning while only the highest closure-valid pointer can be selected. An exact selected retry is idempotent. Missing/corrupt dependencies refuse before selection; an occupied-slot replacement enters the existing buffered retirement owner and hard-flushes its physical incarnation before acknowledgement. Postcommit readback or retirement uncertainty returns a committed receipt rather than claiming preselection failure.
- **Hard-mode evidence:** the first-authority owner exposes one real index hard barrier that flushes and verifies an empty KV baseline, advances only the inactive DatabaseHeader slot and timestamp, and returns the actual `HardAuthority` durability receipt. It preserves HEAD, write-sequence high-water, hot-tail offset, entry count, and every semantic/KV authority field; P6-3c2 can therefore drive the frozen hard-publication state machine without fabricating durability from pointer sequences.
- **Proof and retained boundary:** the new file-backed target first failed on the absent API and then exposed an intentionally incomplete FieldIndex closure; final tests publish the complete Scope/Value/Field chain and retain the missing-parent refusal as a regression. Four focused tests cover A/B/A selection, both-slot exposure, exact retry, replacement retirement, close/reopen, foreign and noncanonical input, incomplete closure, wrong rewrite slot, SHA-512, and hard-barrier identity/failure/preservation behavior. The adjacent first-authority, pointer planner, generation publication, mutable control, and replacement-retirement matrix passes 43 tests with zero failures under one process/thread and ten-second binary timeouts. Workspace all-target checking, Rust formatting, and diff hygiene pass with only the inherited unused `require_wasm_parser` warning. This unit activates no runtime publisher, query path, migration, deployment, or production database; P6-3c2 must compose these primitives through the frozen crash state machine and reconcile every physical prefix before any workspace can rotate.

## P6-3c2 Crash-Resumable Frozen Generation Publication

- **One composed publication authority:** `publish_frozen_index_application_v1` is now the sole bridge from a publication-ready `FrozenIndexBatchApplicationPlanV1` into v4 first authority. It preflights the complete owner graph before mutation, writes the bounded immutable page/directory overlay first, writes every successor manifest in parent-before-child order, optionally crosses one real immutable hard barrier, selects each ScopeCatalog and FieldIndex A/B pointer through the existing retirement owner, optionally crosses one later pointer hard barrier, and rereads every selected closure through the existing publication state machine before returning. ValueStore successors have no independent pointer and are accepted only when a same-batch FieldIndex successor selects them transitively; unreachable ValueStore output refuses before any authority mutation.
- **Retry and failure direction:** an exact selected pointer is reused only when its A/B pair needs no repair; otherwise the canonical rewrite planner repairs the pair. Every error carries one truthful boundary: prior authority retained, pointer commit unknown, or successor pointer visible. Once any pointer can be visible, cancellation, allocation, readback, reconstruction, barrier, or validation failure remains successor-visible reconciliation work. Exact soft retry leaves headers and pointers byte-identical; exact hard retry retains idempotent pointers while producing two new real ordered durability barriers. Partial multi-owner selection is never advertised as batch completion.

The refreshed adjacent executor/pointer/state-machine/applicator/runtime-publisher/retirement matrix passes 84 tests; together the storage perimeter passes 98 tests under one process/thread and ten-second binary watchdogs.
