## P6-3c3 Entry Map

- **Existing producer and retained failure:** `IndexProducerTaskKindV1::Compact` is already one of the nine canonical durable AITK task kinds. `IndexRuntimeOwnerV1::execute_next_producer` leases it through the sole `lease_next` dispatcher, but deliberately converts it to `UnsupportedServiceMode`, releases the lease, latches the runtime degraded, and retains the task. Journal, authoritative-scan, parser, mapper, and collector sources are correctly not invoked. The cadence holds the same mutable runtime publisher lock across dispatch, spill, and due publication.
- **Existing compaction and publication primitives:** P5 owns a pure bounded one/two-page compactor, exact outward Posting relinks, tombstone-drop proof, recursive directory COW, closure validation, immutable overlay limits, and both-width fixtures. P6-3b owns successor-manifest synthesis and the publication-ready owner-plan shape. P6-3c2 is the sole dependency/manifest/A/B pointer executor over `V4FirstAuthorityPublisher` and the shared retirement owner. No production bridge currently selects a compaction window, turns its COW closure into a frozen application plan, or invokes that executor from the retained producer lease.
- **Authority constraints discovered at entry:** compaction must reject a source manifest that is no longer selected before it can replace a newer generation. ScopeCatalog and FieldIndex are pointer-bearing; ValueStore is reachable only through FieldIndex, so a ValueStore compaction must carry the complete bounded set of dependent selected FieldIndex manifests rather than inventing an independent pointer. Coverage is copied exactly, PageIds/ordinals never renumber, unchanged windows complete without publishing, and tombstones remain unless the existing proof-bearing authority permits removal. A selected pointer followed by interrupted task completion remains replay/reconciliation work; it cannot be mistaken for an unstarted task or consumed merely because immutable bytes exist.
- **Execution slices and first red target:** P6-3c3a adds one storage-neutral compaction application outcome (`Unchanged` or the existing frozen publication-ready plan), with selected-source, dependent-manifest, path, bounds, and coverage validation. P6-3c3b routes `Compact` through one injected executor while retaining the exact producer lease through retry/cancel/commit-unknown outcomes. P6-3c3c supplies the native first-authority/memory/retirement adapter under the existing cadence lock. P6-3c3d closes crash/restart, stale source, pressure, corruption, partial-pointer, both-width, architecture, and unchanged-v3 proof. The first compile-red target will be a dedicated storage-neutral compaction applicator spec; the existing unsupported-compaction owner regression will then be converted to exact leased success/failure assertions rather than deleted.

## P6-3c3a Storage-Neutral Compaction Applicator

- **Application closure:** one bounded request now validates the selected source manifest, exact one/two-page window, directory paths, generation, owner role, immutable overlay limits, and every ValueStore-dependent FieldIndex selector before returning either `Unchanged` or the existing publication-ready frozen application plan. The applicator reuses the P5 page compactor, directory COW, closure validator, manifest synthesizer, sparse overlay, and P6-3c2 owner ordering rather than introducing a second artifact or publication format.
- **Authority and coverage:** ScopeCatalog and FieldIndex compaction preserve exact source coverage and produce one pointer-bearing successor. ValueStore compaction has no independent selector, so it requires a strict owner-ordered set of dependent FieldIndex manifests, rewrites each child to the exact ValueStore successor, and emits parent before children. Non-ValueStore dependents, missing/duplicate/stale/foreign children, incompatible coverage, stale generations, and an unowned role fail before a plan exists.
- **Failure/resource proof:** the dedicated target first failed to compile on the absent request/outcome/API. The unchanged frozen-batch applicator passes all 20 regressions, and the first-authority executor passes nine tests including the stale-selected-source refusal that prevents an old compaction plan from replacing a newer selected generation.
- **Retained boundary:** the applicator is storage-neutral and consumes no producer task. It activates no runtime executor, native selector, timer, query path, migration cutover, deployment, or production database. P6-3c3b owns the canonical durable lease and P6-3c3c owns native source selection plus first-authority publication.

## P6-3c3b Canonical Leased Compaction Execution

- **One runtime path:** `Compact` now leaves the retained unsupported branch and enters one injected `IndexRuntimeCompactionExecutorV1` through the existing `lease_next` dispatch. The runtime owner retains the exact lease while the executor runs outside the owner mutex, then reacquires and validates that same lease before any completion transition. `Complete` consumes the task, while a bounded publication returns `Progress`, releases the lease, and retains the task for another source-generation-derived candidate.
- **Failure direction:** retryable preselection failures schedule the existing durable task retry. Preselection cancellation releases the lease without consuming work. Corruption, invalid executor evidence, panic, and commit uncertainty latch the runtime degraded and retain the task. A pointer-visible or commit-unknown result can never be converted to retry-before-selection. Stable code/context and nonzero publication evidence are validated at the owner boundary before worker state changes.
- **Proof:** the runtime-owner suite passes 50 tests, including complete/progress, retry, cancellation, malformed result/error, corruption, panic, pointer uncertainty, exact lease retention, and task recovery. The cadence suite passes 12 tests and the runtime-publisher suite passes 29 tests, proving the same cadence lock and producer owner remain the only service/publication doorway.

## P6-3c3c Native First-Authority Adapter

- **Bounded discovery:** one native executor resolves the exact maintenance-scope semantic inventory, reads only selected ScopeCatalog and FieldIndex pointers plus transitively selected ValueStore manifests, and chooses at most one deterministic adjacent-page candidate per ordered role. No selector, timer, writer, cache, or publication format was added.
- **Operational classification:** missing/malformed/identity-invalid immutable authority is corruption; allocation, bounded-path pressure, native I/O, shutdown, and durability transport refusal are retryable only before pointer selection; cancellation is cancellable only before selection; and any pointer-commit uncertainty or visible successor remains `CommitUnknown`. ## P6-3d Workspace-Rotation Entry Map

- **Authority map:** `DurableIndexRuntimeBatchPublisherV1` is the only writer of cumulative AIWM/AIWO workspace state and the only caller of the existing `IndexOperationControl` selector. `IndexProducerCoordinatorV1` is the only live pending-task/completion owner. Dirty-overlay recovery streams every selected historical AITK object into that coordinator, so the current append-only chain is deliberately not a completion ledger and restart work grows with all historical tasks. Runtime batches share the same chain and are validated on reopen but must remain available until selected immutable generation coverage proves them represented.
- **Persistent constraint:** AIWM v1 is a cumulative chain and cannot be pruned in place. Rotation therefore creates a side-by-side private workspace with the same database/runtime family identity, copies only unresolved batches and exact pending tasks, writes one immutable runtime checkpoint, and advances the existing selector last. The prior path remains intact across every uncertain prefix. Frozen checkpoint bytes already distinguish the global checkpoint sequence from the external workspace's local durable sequence; production code currently equates them and must stop doing so. An empty successor must be represented explicitly rather than retaining a fake anchor task or batch.
- **Coverage and pending-work contract:** a selected immutable coverage proof may discard a runtime batch only when the complete batch publication interval is represented. A historical producer task absent from the coordinator's exact pending set may be discarded only when immutable coverage represents its publication sequence. Every pending operation identity must match one selected workspace task and is carried once; unknown, conflicting, straddling, incomplete, or foreign evidence refuses rotation before selection.
- **Proof split:** P6-3d1 adds the bounded inventory/classification contract and compile-red exact-state target. P6-3d2 writes and reopens the side-by-side successor with only retained work. P6-3d3 routes rotation through the existing publisher/selector and supports clean empty recovery while preserving global checkpoint monotonicity. P6-3d4 exhausts crash prefixes, exact retry, cancellation, malformed objects/manifests, memory/disk pressure, both hash widths, restart, and architecture proof that no second selector, timer, replay owner, or writer exists.
- **First falsifier:** select a workspace containing represented and unresolved runtime batches plus completed and pending producer tasks. Rotate against an exact immutable coverage frontier and coordinator pending set, then restart from every object/checkpoint/selector prefix. The old selector must always recover the old complete chain; the successor selector must recover exactly unresolved batches and pending tasks; no state may replay completed history, advertise partial coverage, or require scanning objects retired before the selected rotated head.

## P6-3d1 Bounded Rotation Inventory

- **Red-first contract:** the dedicated rotation target first failed to compile because no workspace-rotation contract existed. One storage-neutral streaming planner now binds the exact runtime ID, generation, source NamespaceRoot, nonzero coverage epoch, selected object count, and selected immutable coverage frontier before observing an object. It requires canonical pending operation identities, strict manifest sequence, valid publication intervals, and one exact pending-task match.
- **Bounded classification:** fully represented runtime batches and completed producer tasks may be discarded. A batch that crosses or follows the frontier is retained whole. Pending tasks following the frontier are retained exactly once; a pending task already covered, an absent pending task, a duplicate pending identity, or a nonpending task beyond coverage refuses rotation. Planner memory is proportional only to the pending task count, not historical objects. The 10,000-completed-plus-two-pending model retains exactly two objects.
- **Native inventory binding:** `DurableIndexRuntimeWorkspaceV1::plan_rotation` first requires exact physical object and manifest inventories, then rereads the selected AIWM chain oldest-first and revalidates every manifest, AIWO header, payload checksum/digest, runtime semantic closure, and producer-task payload before feeding the planner. Canonical unselected manifests or objects fail closed. Cumulative bytes and the final selected digest must close exactly. Producer payload memory remains admitted through callback completion and returns to its pre-inventory level on success and refusal.
- **Evidence:** eight planner tests cover exact/partial frontiers, 10,000 historical completions, pending/completed disagreement, missing and duplicate tasks, malformed ordering/intervals, constructor drift, cancellation at construction/observation/finalization, and both BLAKE3/SHA-512 widths. Thirty-three real workspace tests include file-backed classification and an unselected-manifest falsifier; all 29 runtime-publisher regressions and all 29 error-suppression architecture tests pass. Package all-target checking passes serially with only the inherited unused `require_wasm_parser` warning. No writer, selector, checkpoint, runtime activation, query path, deployment, or production database changes in this unit.
- **Next:** P6-3d2 writes a side-by-side successor from these same validated decisions, preserving unresolved runtime payloads and exactly pending producer tasks without buffering a 512 MiB object or mutating the selected predecessor.

## P6-3d2 Side-by-Side Successor Writer

- **One-pass construction:** `build_rotation_successor` reuses the P6-3d1 streaming classifier and creates one deterministic private sibling keyed by the future global rotation sequence. A selected `-r<sequence>` predecessor may advance only to a greater sequence, and repeated rotations resolve against the canonical base name instead of growing suffix chains. The predecessor descriptor and files remain read-only throughout.
- **Bounded byte preservation:** retained AIWO payloads are copied without decoding them into an aggregate buffer. The source object is first fully validated by the existing semantic reader, then reopened no-follow and copied through one fixed 64 KiB window while CRC32, payload BLAKE3, whole-object BLAKE3, length, identity, and final metadata are rechecked. The successor receives a newly encoded local-sequence header, immutable object, and cumulative manifest through the existing durability primitives. Planner memory remains proportional to pending identities and temporary copy memory is object-size independent.
- **Exact retry and empty state:** object-before-manifest prefixes, fully written prefixes, and complete successors are byte-exact retryable. Existing conflicts, extra manifests/objects, stale or same rotation sequences, cancellation, and source drift fail before any selector change. A fully represented predecessor produces an exact private directory skeleton with no synthetic object or head; P6-3d3 will publish and recover that clean state explicitly.
- **Evidence:** the store target first failed to compile on the absent successor API and now passes 36 file-backed tests. New cases preserve unresolved runtime and pending-task payload bytes, discard represented history, recover only one pending task, retry a missing final manifest, rotate `r9` to sibling `r10`, refuse same/older/zero sequences, preserve exact empty successors, reject a corrupt target prefix, preserve predecessor bytes, release temporary memory, and prove the copy path contains no `fs::read`/`read_to_end`. All 29 runtime-publisher regressions and all 29 error-suppression architecture tests pass; serial package all-target checking, formatting, and diff validation pass with only the inherited unused `require_wasm_parser` warning.
- **Retained boundary:** no checkpoint, selector, active runtime installation, query authority, migration state, cleanup, deployment, or production database changed. P6-3d3 owns global/local sequence decoupling, selector-last publication, selected empty-workspace recovery, and checkpoint crash reconciliation.

## P6-3d3 Selector-Last Rotation and Restart Recovery

- **One publication authority:** rotation now builds the deterministic P6-3d2 successor, validates its complete selected closure, persists one ordinary immutable runtime checkpoint, and advances the existing `IndexOperationControl` selector last. The publisher retains the exact candidate workspace until selection is proven, leaves the predecessor active on definite refusal, and swaps in-memory ownership only after direct selector success or exact checkpoint/workspace reopen following an uncertain result. Ordinary batches, producer tasks, and rotations share the same pending-publication state machine and selector call.
- **Global/local sequence contract:** checkpoint sequence and operation `completed_work` remain globally monotonic across rotation, while `external.durable_sequence` is the independently reset workspace-local object/manifest sequence. The native first-authority test initially rejected local progress 1 after global progress 2, exposing the hidden coupling. The writer and recovery validator now preserve operation progress 1, 2, 3 while a retained successor may restart locally at 1 and continue at 2. Existing pre-rotation checkpoints remain compatible because their global and local sequences were identical.
- **Exact clean recovery:** a fully represented workspace publishes a checkpoint with global progress and no external descriptor. Identity-aware recovery derives the deterministic `-r<global-sequence>` sibling from the configured database/runtime identity and opens only an already durable exact empty directory skeleton. It does not fabricate a task, batch, directory, manifest, or object; contextless recovery and unexpected retained state fail closed. Nonempty recovery similarly revalidates the exact configured workspace identity and local descriptor rather than equating it to global operation progress.
- **Proof:** the exact formatted source passes 34 runtime-publisher tests, including nonempty and empty rotation/restart, definite selector refusal and exact retry, commit-then-error reconciliation, native first-authority selection/reopen, global/local continuation, and one-selector architecture proof. Adjacent targets pass 36 workspace-store, 8 rotation-model, 10 coverage-runtime, 37 migration/installation, 29 error-squelch architecture, 2 internal installation, and 14 internal cadence tests. `cargo check --locked -p aeordb --all-targets -j 1`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass; the only compile warning is the inherited unused `require_wasm_parser` test macro.
- **Retained boundary:** the prior workspace remains physically intact and no retirement, cleanup, query authority, migration cutover, deployment, or production database changed. P6-3d4 next exhausts every object/checkpoint/selector crash prefix, cancellation point, corruption and pressure refusal, both hash widths, old-head retention, exact retry, and restart replay bound before the rotation phase closes.

## P6-3d4 Fault, Crash, and Bounded-Replay Closure

- **Exact crash retry:** cancellation after immutable checkpoint sync but before selector publication now restarts from the old selected workspace and reuses the exact already durable checkpoint and deterministic successor path for both nonempty and fully represented empty rotations. Rotation adds no logical work, so its checkpoint retains the selected authority timestamp instead of sampling a new wall clock on retry. This closes byte-exact immutable identity across process restart without weakening ordinary publication timestamps.
- **Failure direction:** immutable-sync refusal retains one exact retry; corrupt successor objects or manifests fail before the selector; selector commit followed by missing checkpoint, unreadable selection, or foreign selection remains `CommitUnknown` and cannot swap the in-memory predecessor. Successful selection leaves the physical predecessor bytes and manifests intact. Existing object-before-manifest, missing-final-manifest, cancellation-during-inventory, source-drift, and target-conflict cases remain exact and nonmutating.
- **Bounds and recovery:** real file-backed pressure exhausts shared workload admission before successor installation, leaves predecessor bytes and memory accounting unchanged, and succeeds after reservation release. A 66-task selected chain with 64 covered completions rotates to exactly two pending task objects and restart streams exactly those two identities; the storage-neutral 10,002-task model independently proves memory and replay scale with pending work rather than completed history. BLAKE3-256 and SHA3-512 selection and restart preserve the exact hash width.
- **Architecture:** production source retains one `publish_selected_synced` selector call, one rotation publisher, one successor-workspace writer, one dirty-overlay recovered-task owner, and one empty-rotation recovery adapter. Publisher, recovery, and workspace modules contain no independent async or thread spawn. The production coordinator remains the canonical recovered-task ordering owner; workspace callbacks are validated as an exact identity set rather than inventing a second execution-order authority.
- **Proof:** 40 runtime-publisher, 38 workspace-store, 8 rotation-model, 10 coverage-runtime, 37 migration/installation, 29 error-squelch architecture, 2 internal installation, and 12 internal cadence tests pass under direct ten-second binary watchdogs and one test thread. `cargo check --workspace --all-targets -j 1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `git diff --check` pass; the only compile warning is the inherited unused `require_wasm_parser` test macro.
- **Retained boundary:** no old workspace is deleted, no lifecycle cleanup or retirement is activated, and v3 query behavior, migration cutover, deployment, and production databases remain unchanged. P6-3e next installs the selected-generation coverage registry and exact complete/partial/degraded/no-index planning contract; P6-3f owns migration-qualified runtime activation and workspace lifecycle integration.

## P6-3e Selected-Generation Coverage Entry Map

- **Producers and authority:** selected ScopeCatalog and FieldIndex A/B pointers are the only generation selectors; each selected manifest carries an exact source `NamespaceRoot`, coverage epoch/publication sequence, immutable definition bytes, and transitive parent-manifest closure. The selected `NamespaceRoot` identifies its exact semantic state. Runtime lifecycle/degraded state may restrict use but cannot strengthen coverage. FieldNvt is selected independently and is only a disposable hint over one exact FieldIndex generation/source root.
- **Consumers:** P7 query/search/filter/sort/aggregate/EXPLAIN will consume one generation plan under a resolved and pinned read root. P6-3f will install and refresh the registry at migration-qualified runtime activation. Existing v3 `IndexManager` query/search authority remains unchanged until that coordinated cutover; P6-3e may not add a shadow live query route.
- **Exactness contract:** an exact-root, definition-compatible, closure-valid immutable generation may answer completely even when the mutable runtime is degraded. A healthy older compatible generation is usable only with a bounded exact changed-document complement, exact recheck of accelerator candidates against the requested root/revision, and FileKey/revision dedupe. A degraded older generation, absent/corrupt/incompatible generation, or unavailable complement is ignored in favor of bounded authoritative evaluation. Content-only or dependency-unavailable historical semantics return typed `HISTORICAL_VIEW_UNAVAILABLE`; they are never evaluated with current definitions.
- **Execution split:** P6-3e1 freezes the storage-neutral decision algebra and adversarial model. P6-3e2 loads closure-valid selected generations into one bounded immutable registry without pages. P6-3e3 binds authoritative-complement/recheck/dedupe requirements and exact Posting-directory fallback while keeping NVT advisory. P6-3e4 closes pointer-change/restart/corruption/cancellation/pressure/both-width/in-flight-retention and architecture proof. P6-3f/P7 retain actual runtime/query activation.
- **First falsifier:** give the planner an apparently healthy selected generation whose manifest source root precedes the requested root but no exact complement witness. It must choose authoritative-only evaluation, not partial index results. Supplying an exact bounded complement must require both candidate recheck and dedupe before partial acceleration can become eligible. A stale or corrupt NVT must never change either decision.

## P6-3e1 Storage-Neutral Coverage Planner

- **Decision algebra:** one pure planner distinguishes complete immutable coverage, a non-authoritative partial candidate, authoritative-only evaluation, and historical semantic unavailability. Exact source-root/owner/definition/dependency compatibility is required before a selected generation can contribute. An exact-root immutable generation remains complete while its mutable runtime is degraded; a degraded older generation is ignored. Content-only and unavailable-dependency views return typed historical unavailability before any current semantics can be borrowed.
- **Partial proof obligations:** P6-3e3 tightened the provisional P6-3e1 algebra: an older healthy generation becomes only `PartialCandidate`, carrying the selected generation and target root/sequence into the exact executor. The planner cannot accept caller-fabricated complement evidence and the generation alone is never result authority. The executor must independently complete the bounded complement, candidate recheck, and deduplication obligations before it can construct the opaque exact proof.
- **NVT and activation boundary:** the planner has no NVT input or dependency. NVT cannot strengthen coverage or suppress Posting-directory fallback. This unit adds no storage reader, registry cache, pointer observer, runtime installation, query route, migration activation, deployment, or production database mutation.
- **Proof:** the target first failed to compile on the absent module. Eight focused tests now cover exact and partial roots, healthy/degraded state, both hash widths, absent and incompatible generations, complement bounds, malformed request/generation/proof shapes, content-only/dependency-unavailable views, and a source architecture gate excluding NVT. Existing coverage-runtime, coverage-journal, and v4 read-view targets pass 10, 12, and 19 tests. `cargo check --workspace --all-targets -j 1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `git diff --check` pass with only the inherited unused `require_wasm_parser` warning.
- **Next:** P6-3e2 loads selected ScopeCatalog, FieldIndex, and optional compatible FieldNvt closure into one bounded immutable registry while preserving the existing A/B selector as sole authority.

## P6-3e2 Bounded Selected-Generation Registry

- **Authority and bounds:** one nonblocking refresh owner now projects the existing first-authority A/B selection into an immutable `Arc` snapshot under `IndexCleanCache` admission. Refresh reserves before every bounded pointer/manifest load, retains only compact authority/coverage/fingerprint/NVT metadata, and atomically swaps only after cancellation and exact pointer-pair recheck. Old snapshots and their reservations remain live until the final in-flight reader releases them.

Cancellation still aborts the whole refresh. The registry contains no page decoder, pointer writer, thread/task spawn, query route, or second selector.

Eleven focused tests now cover empty authority, both hash widths, exact planner handoff, missing/corrupt/repair-required pairs, zero coverage, dishonest source shape, transitive corruption, pointer movement, recursive/concurrent refresh refusal, cancellation, process-memory pressure, request ordering/count/width, NVT absence/staleness/source failure, in-flight generation retention, and architecture uniqueness. Workspace all-target checking, formatting, and diff hygiene pass with only the inherited unused `require_wasm_parser` test macro warning.

## P6-3e3 Exact Partial-Acceleration Proof

- **Authority and type-state:** the planner now emits only a non-authoritative `PartialCandidate`. The storage-neutral executor is the sole constructor of `ExactPartialIndexAccelerationV1` and its opaque `ExactIndexComplementProofV1`, after a complete selected-generation candidate scan, a complete strict-FileKey immutable-root diff, selected-root rechecks, and FileKey/revision deduplication all succeed. Architecture checks keep NVT, storage/runtime owners, routes, and task/thread spawning out of the planner and executor.

Candidate unavailability/resource pressure/corruption, complement unavailability/resource pressure, recheck unavailability/resource pressure, result limits, and ordinary local allocation pressure return typed authoritative-only fallbacks. Malformed or incomplete authoritative complement evidence and corrupt selected-root rechecks fail closed; cancellation is checked before work, at both source receipts (including empty streams), during rechecks, and before exact return. Candidate revision conflicts are disposable acceleration corruption, while a candidate revision missing from the exact changed set proves complement corruption.

## P7-1b2 Bounded Root-Lifecycle Point Reader

- **Authority and baseline:** `V4FirstAuthorityPublisher::observe_root_lifecycle_at_captured_header` holds the existing root-state publication lock, validates captured/current database and physical-instance identity, treats current HEAD as live, and treats two absent lifecycle controls as the retained pre-lifecycle baseline. Immutable namespace authority remains bound to the captured header while the lifecycle selector intentionally comes from the current header.
- **Bounded traversal:** the reader reuses the existing A/B lifecycle selector and shared GC directory child/page validators. It reads one selected lifecycle manifest, optional expiry manifest, and only the descriptor path whose inclusive fences contain the requested root. It retains at most one parent plus one selected child, binary-searches one fixed-width page, and never reads unrelated sibling pages. Selected malformed, incomplete, ambiguous, foreign, cross-role, or candidate/expiry-conflicting authority fails closed; malformed unselected siblings cannot poison a valid point lookup.
- **State and resources:** point lookup distinguishes retained, pending-delete, logically retired, and physically reclaimed state. Pending state returns both the persisted grace-at-mark and the current configured grace so the existing read-view owner computes the non-shortening effective expiry dynamically. Selector and entity allocations reserve `MemoryOwner::Query` before reads; exact release is proven on success, pressure refusal, corruption, conflict, and cancellation. The accounting path returns a typed overflow failure rather than converting failure to absence.
- **Proof:** the freshly compiled native target passes 6 real-file tests at BLAKE3-256 and SHA-512. The complete library harness passes 592 tests, including dynamic grace, absent-root retention, selected-page corruption, unselected-sibling corruption, tiny-budget refusal, conflicting candidate/expiry state, guarded logical retirement, and physical reclaim. Workspace all-target checking passes serially with only the inherited unused test macro warning. All 29 suppression architecture tests pass against an exact 1,532-site reviewed inventory; this reader adds no suppression. Formatting, diff hygiene, mdBook, and the independent 454-fixture/95-route/38-document contract gate pass.
- **Retained boundary and next:** this disconnected adapter adds no HTTP/SDK route, request pin, permission decision, task/thread, selector, writer, persistent format, migration activation, deployment, or production database operation. P7-1b3 next extracts the shared permission evaluator and implements current authorization intersected with selected-root restrictions; P7-1b4 then owns the combined request pin, race, concealment, pressure, and architecture proof.

## P7-1b3 Permission Adapter Entry Territory

- **Current authority:** ordinary requests first apply active-account, API-key rule, share-key, revocation, protected-family, and route concealment policy in middleware. Share credentials use current API-key rules as sole permission authority and may select only current HEAD. Ordinary user/API-key requests then evaluate current group membership through `PermissionResolver`; root bypass remains current authority and does not bypass protected-family policy.
- **Ordered documents:** `PermissionResolver::check_direct_permission` currently owns the only root-to-target `.aeordb-permissions` inheritance evaluator. The current cache loader supplies optional typed documents, while malformed stored bytes are `CorruptEntry`. Path-form fallback, ancestor navigation, and immediate-child filtering are separate current adapters through the global grants index. HTTP middleware, route helpers, sync, SSE, plugins, fetch/download/share/version handlers, and effective-permission reporting consume these adapters.
- **Target boundary:** P7-1b3 will extract the ordered document fold into one storage-neutral evaluator and make the current resolver delegate to it without changing current results. One request-owned current snapshot will retain the normalized path, operation, current group identity, direct-or-filtered decision, credential/concealment context, and whether selected document restriction applies. A storage-neutral selected-root source boundary will return a decision for the captured immutable authority; the request authorizer will intersect decisions and can only remove access or child names.
- **Failure direction:** current denial returns before header/root/lifecycle/selected-document access. Selected denial cannot become current allow; empty filtered intersections deny. Cancellation, unavailable selected authority, and corrupt selected permission authority remain distinct typed failures. Root/share current-only decisions are explicit, ordinary API/user requests reuse current group identity, and no historical membership or permission document may expand current access.
- **Landing boundary:** this unit adds no native selected-tree walker, route integration, service activation, task/thread, persistent format, writer, migration, deployment, or production database operation. P7-1b4 owns the real-file selected permission source, combined resolver races/pins/concealment/pressure, and architecture uniqueness; P7-1c remains the only HTTP/embedded activation boundary.

## P7-1b3 Permission Adapter Evidence

- **One permission algebra:** `PermissionResolver::check_direct_permission` now delegates exactly once to the storage-neutral `evaluate_ordered_path_permissions` fold. The fold preserves root-to-target inheritance, same-level deny precedence, later-level overrides, exact path-pattern matching, `others_*` behavior, leading-slash normalization, and directory/file path forms. Removing the old filename default reduced the reviewed error-squelch inventory from 1,532 to 1,531 sites instead of adding a replacement suppression.
- **Restrictive request authority:** `CurrentPathAuthorizationV1` retains the normalized path, operation, current group identity, direct-or-filtered decision, and explicit user/root/share selected-root policy. `ReadViewPermissionAuthorizerV1` evaluates current authority first, rejects credential/policy disagreement before selected-root access, skips selected documents only for explicit current-only root/share policy, and intersects ordinary selected-root decisions. The selected source never receives the current decision and therefore cannot union or expand it; disjoint filtered child sets deny.
- **Failure and cancellation direction:** current denial, corruption, or unavailability prevents all selected-root permission access and retains route concealment. Selected denial, corruption, unavailability, and cancellation remain distinct. Cancellation is checked before and after selected-source work, and the generic resolver maps selected cancellation to the common `read_view_canceled` failure without exposing selected-root authority.

## P7-1b4 Combined Native Resolver Entry Territory

- **Persisted producers:** v4 migration/replay publishes ordinary immutable `DirectoryIndex` v0 entities in canonical flat or B-tree form, `FileRecord` v0/v1 entities, and `Chunk` v0 entities, then binds one namespace-tree identity into an admitted `NamespaceRootV1`. Permission authority is an ordinary bounded `.aeordb-permissions` JSON file at each directory level. The active header, immutable admission witness, current lifecycle controls, and GC request-pin coordinator are separate authorities and must not be inferred from entity presence.
- **Current consumers:** `ReadViewResolverV1` is still disconnected from services and has fake-source coverage. `V4FirstAuthorityPublisher` owns the only native v4 file/KV/header/lifecycle authority; migration replay contains a private destination path lookup for mutation projection, while the v3 index source contains a separate current-engine reader and is not valid selected-v4 authority. P7-1c remains the only HTTP/embedded construction and activation owner.
- **Race correction:** the generic resolver currently loads immutable authority and invokes selected-root authorization before lifecycle admission acquires the request pin. That ordering leaves later namespace pages vulnerable to physical reclamation. P7-1b4 must authorize current credentials first, capture one header/root, admit lifecycle and acquire the pin, and only then read immutable authority or selected permission bytes. Every later failure drops the pin; every successful `ResolvedReadViewV1` retains it until drop.
- **Resource contract:** native authority and permission reads must reserve `MemoryOwner::Query` before allocation through the same process coordinator used by request pins. The retained authority reservation lives with the resolved view; transient directory/FileRecord/chunk permission workspace remains bounded and releases per read. Pressure refusal, arithmetic overflow, malformed entities, cancellation, and missing required closure never become absence or permission denial.
- **Permission semantics:** direct selected authority reuses the P7-1b3 ordered evaluator. If direct read/list is denied, selected ancestor navigation mirrors the current grants-index behavior: scan a bounded selected subtree for permission documents, consider exact current-group links regardless of their operation flags, and return only immediate child names leading to strict descendant grant targets. The selected source cannot receive or expand the current decision; root/share current-only paths perform no selected namespace read.
- **Proof boundary:** extend the existing real native v4 target at both hash widths. Cover flat and B-tree point reads, v0/v1 FileRecords, chunk/content/path validation, descendant scan bounds, current-denial concealment, current-only policy, concurrent HEAD advance, physical/regression drift, cancellation during reads, corruption, memory refusal/release, retirement exclusion, drop-time release, and source/adapter uniqueness. This unit adds no route, service activation, writer format, migration execution, deployment, or production database operation.
- **Pin-order proof:** the generic resolver now performs lifecycle admission and acquires the request pin immediately after current authorization, one header capture, semantic admission, selector validation, and share-current enforcement. Immutable authority and selected permission reads occur only under that pin; unknown roots, source failures, selected denial/corruption, cancellation, closure validation, and capability refusal all release it. The focused resolver target passes 19/19 tests and the root-bound transfer consumer passes 7/7 tests.

## P7-2 APOS and Total-Order Entry Territory

- **Entry and split:** P7-2 enters at pushed green commit `98f77a7`. Query, fuzzy search, and aggregate sorting each own separate partial comparators. P7-2 must introduce one logical contract without deleting or silently activating these compatibility paths before P7-5/P8.

It carries no authorization, expiry, window origin, limit, physical offset, index/NVT state, or planner details.

Later slices add model-based order/window proofs and real selected-root resolution without using the production serializer as their oracle.

## P7-2b Logical Total-Order and Window Entry

- **Entry and ownership:** P7-2b enters at pushed green commit `a3d4595`. It owns a storage-neutral logical row/order comparator, the four route-owned order builders, exactly-one-origin pagination validation, and a bounded source/visitor page-window contract. It does not resolve APOS against a selected root, read an index page, activate a route schema, replace the legacy v3 cursor, or choose a physical planner.
- **Territory:** current directory listing, query sorting/cursors, global fuzzy search, and aggregation each own a separate partial comparator and materialize complete result vectors before pagination. Future producers are P7-3 exact index/authoritative scan adapters and aggregate execution; consumers are P7-2c APOS revalidation and the coordinated P7-5 HTTP/embedded/client cutover. The existing v3 paths remain behaviorally untouched until that coordinated boundary.
- **Frozen model:** route builders include every ordering policy in the order fingerprint. Directory rows keep category ascending regardless requested direction, then the requested primary key, canonical folded/raw name and path ties. Query rows use declared typed fields or canonical path ascending, with present before typed null before missing and direction reversal only for present values. Global search uses finite score descending then canonical path; aggregates use declared group order or count descending then canonical group tuple. FileKey and RecordRevision remain final ascending ties outside the tuple.
- **Bounded execution:** page and offset compile to checked absolute ranks; after/before compile to strict logical-bound seeks that P7-2c must first resolve. A source must seek directly by rank/bound and stream in requested or reverse order. The model retains no more than `limit + 1` rows under `query.position_scan_buffer_bytes`, reverses only the bounded `before` page, and rejects incomplete receipts, extra rows, malformed/duplicate/out-of-order rows, cancellation, or resource refusal.
- **Proof:** add an independent deterministic model source/comparator before production code. It must cover every typed comparator and presence state, directories-first ascending/descending and category crossing, complete query/fuzzy/aggregate ties, all legal/illegal origins, arithmetic and byte boundaries, forward/reverse windows, rank seeks, empty/singleton sets, cancellation, and adversarial source receipts.

## P7-2b Logical Total-Order and Window Completion

- **One permanent order:** four route-owned builders now compile directory, query, global-search, and aggregate orders through the sole canonical order compiler. Their fingerprints bind permanent typed comparators and route policies, including directories-first behavior independent of direction, the exact Unicode 17 fold-table fingerprint, present/null/missing ordering, min/max multi-value selection, corrected finite-score semantics, canonical path/group ties, and final FileKey/RecordRevision identity ties. Complete rows reject route, arity, identity-width, zero-identity, state, comparator, payload, category, and policy drift before comparison.
- **Bounded window contract:** exactly one planner admits start, one-based page, zero-based offset, exclusive after, or exclusive before origins and rejects combinations, zero/out-of-range limits, and checked-rank overflow. Exactly one storage-neutral executor asks its source for a direct rank/bound seek, visits at most `limit + 1` rows, accounts conservative retained bytes, normalizes only the bounded reverse `before` window, checks cancellation before/during/after the scan, and rejects extra, duplicate, out-of-order, wrong-bound, dishonest-receipt, and incomplete streams. It does not sort or materialize a skipped prefix and has no storage, server, task, or legacy-query dependency.
- **Adversarial correction:** the error-suppression gate caught eleven provisional shortcuts in the new comparator/window implementation. They were removed rather than reviewed into the inventory: fixed-width decodes now return typed corruption errors, UTF-8 retains its failure context, finite floats use validated `total_cmp`, and absent optional limits use an explicit branch. The complete 29-test suppression architecture target passes with the unchanged 1,531-site reviewed ceiling; only existing line metadata was refreshed after prior P7 source insertions.

## P7-2c Authorized Position Resolution Entry

- **Entry and territory:** P7-2c enters at pushed green commit `9d350e1`. The frozen APOS reader can validate framing and caller-supplied context, while P7-2b can validate and compare complete logical rows, but no owner yet proves that a token names an actual row in one authorized selected-root result universe. Existing v3 cursors remain active compatibility behavior and must not be reused as proof.
- **Authority boundary:** resolution must receive an already-authorized `ResolvedReadViewV1`, preserving its request pin and captured physical/database identity. An explicit selected root, exact database hash algorithm, route, and order fingerprint are checked before invoking a result-universe source. The source receives a privately constructed bounded lookup request and must resolve immutable FileKey/RecordRevision identity, or aggregate synthetic identity, inside that exact universe; token tuple bytes are never lookup authority.
- **Recomputation contract:** the source returns one recomputed complete logical row or absence. The resolver validates route shape, permanent comparators/states/payloads, both immutable ties, configured retained-byte bounds, and exact component equality against the decoded APOS. Absent or tuple-stale tokens are invalid positions; a source returning foreign identities or malformed rows is internal corruption. Aggregate resolution uses the same contract with canonical group tuple and synthetic identities rather than pretending groups are files.
- **First falsifier:** construct a canonical token whose root/order/identity all match an authorized view but whose sort tuple was altered and CRC recomputed. A source that resolves the true row must cause `INVALID_POSITION_CURSOR`, not return a seek bound. Wrong root/order/route and non-explicit views must fail before the source is called; cancellation and resource refusal must retain typed non-success behavior.

## P7-3 Root-Aware Query Planner Entry

- **Entry and split:** P7-3 enters at pushed green commit `fa2a1cb`. P7-3a owns the storage-neutral typed AST, exact selected-definition literal compiler, conservative coordinate spans, measured-cost selection, and logical EXPLAIN model. P7-3b owns one shared bounded selected-root namespace reader. P7-3c owns one shared immutable ArtifactDirectory/page cursor and validated NVT fallback. P7-3d composes exact complete, partial-plus-complement, and authoritative execution with bounded ordering and aggregates. P7-3e closes authorization-safe EXPLAIN and architecture/native proof. Public schemas remain P7-5 and v4 service activation remains P8-only.
- **Territory:** future raw query producers are HTTP and embedded SDK adapters; authoritative inputs are an already-authorized `ResolvedReadViewV1`, its exact semantic root, selected scope/ValueStore/FieldIndex definitions, the bounded coverage registry, and immutable page/value authorities. Consumers are query, global search, sort/top-K, aggregate, P7-2 position windows/resolution, P7-4 locators, P7-5 public schemas, and P8 activation. Current v3 `QueryEngine`, `json_query_value_to_bytes`, cursors, route responses, and mutable index manager remain compatibility behavior until coordinated cutover.
- **Single-path constraints:** query literals are typed values, not route-produced byte guesses. Every accepted leaf compiles through the exact selected-root definition and permanent operation registry. Effective scopes are partitioned explicitly. Complete index coverage, exact partial acceleration, and authoritative fallback remain distinct type states; NVT coordinates and physical spans are hints only. Driver choice uses bounded page/posting/cardinality/coverage/fallback estimates rather than expression order or coordinate width.
- **Security and resources:** current authorization and selected-root restriction precede all observables. Logical EXPLAIN may expose requested fields, operations, coverage class, and relative work estimates only after authorization; it never exposes hidden paths/counts/values, physical offsets, page keys, NVT cells, manifests, or raw cardinalities. Admission enforces depth 32, 1,024 AST nodes, 4,096 IN literals, 1 MiB per literal, 8 MiB total literals, 32 sort fields, 32 fields per aggregate/group family, explicit fuzzy edits at most 8, finite similarity in `[0,1]`, and at most 1,000 returned documents.
- **First falsifier:** submit an `@hash eq` string and an ordered numeric comparison through exact selected definitions, alongside a deceptively narrow but incomplete generation and a wider complete generation. The compiler must produce raw hash bytes and typed numeric posting keys, reject legacy byte coercion and malformed bounds, and the planner must choose the measured exact plan without ever promoting the incomplete generation or coordinate width to correctness authority.

## P7-3a Bounded Root-Aware Query Planner Completion

- **Typed contract and selected semantics:** the compile-red target initially failed on the absent planner module. One storage-neutral planner now validates a bounded boolean AST, canonical path and field names, result/sort/aggregate/group limits, fuzzy parameters, per-literal and aggregate literal bytes, and exact selected-root catalog receipts before compilation. It normalizes the legacy `@file_name` spelling to canonical `@filename`, compiles full-width BLAKE3-256 and SHA-512 `@hash` literals as typed bytes, and routes every accepted predicate, sort, aggregate, and group operation through the exact selected ValueStore/FieldIndex runtime. The compiled plan retains the exact database, physical instance, namespace root, semantic root, publication sequence, normalized typed operation, selected generation, scope/candidate identities, auxiliary operation details, query path, result limit, and boolean expression needed by the executor.
- **Coverage, coordinates, and cost:** complete, exact-partial, and authoritative-only candidates remain distinct. Historical semantic absence is a typed non-success; incompatible, missing, or degraded acceleration cannot claim an empty result. Exact coordinates and endpoint-widening instructions are navigation hints only. Text strategies become drivers only for mathematically proven supersets, including complete phonetic branch unions. Driver choice includes bounded page, posting, distinct-document, candidate, complement, and authoritative work estimates. Advisory estimate arithmetic saturates at `u64::MAX` instead of rejecting an otherwise executable query, and unusable candidates cannot trigger estimate overflow. NVT availability has no effect on coverage or plan equality.
- **Authorization-safe diagnostics and memory boundary:** logical EXPLAIN exposes only requested field, operation, logical driver/coverage/work class, and exact-recheck status; it contains no hidden path/value/count, physical offset, page, NVT, manifest, or raw cardinality. The planner enforces frozen protocol maxima of depth 32, 1,024 nodes, 4,096 IN literals, 1 MiB per literal, 8 MiB total literals, 32 sort/aggregate/group fields, fuzzy edits at most eight, and 1,000 results. Compiled literal bytes are retained once rather than duplicated. P7-3b must reserve source catalog construction before allocating up to the planner caps, and P7-5 must reserve raw request AST/schema construction before handing it to this planner; this pure disconnected unit cannot itself admit allocations made by those future producers.
- **Proof:** 16 focused tests cover exact coercion and aliases, both hash widths, numeric ranges and neighbor widening, deduplicated IN values, all protocol bounds, malformed roots/catalogs/definitions/order, unsupported operations, finite similarity/fuzzy limits, complete/partial/degraded/no-generation coverage, NVT non-authority, mathematically safe text candidates, measured drivers, saturating estimate extremes, cancellation/resource refusal, auxiliary planning, authorization-safe EXPLAIN, and architecture isolation. The adjacent planner/index/coverage/position/suppression matrix passes 127 tests, and the common library harness passes 592/592 under one test thread with disk-backed scratch. Serial workspace all-target checking passes with only the inherited unused `require_wasm_parser` test macro warning. Strict library Clippy remains at the exact inherited 120-diagnostic baseline with no planner finding; retained log SHA-256 remains `8155e6ccd0839b222e4cc63a2d050fc98432e4de63016f98fc54121a349584c7`. Formatting, diff hygiene, mdBook, and the independent 454-fixture/95-route/38-document contract gate pass.
- **Retained boundary and next:** this unit adds no native namespace/catalog reader, artifact/page cursor, query executor, server or embedded schema, legacy-query replacement, migration activation, persistent byte, deployment, or production database access. A real-world service run cannot exercise this disconnected planner and remains attached to P7-3e/P7-5 activation. P7-3b next implements the one bounded native selected-root namespace reader for FileKey/revision/path rows, authoritative source values, and APOS result universes.

## P7-3b Native Selected-Root Reader Entry

- **Entry and territory:** P7-3b enters at pushed green commit `1860853`. `ResolvedReadViewV1` retains the authorized immutable root authority, database/physical/hash identities, header slot/sequence/high-water fields, cancellation token, and request pin, but currently discards the exact captured `SelectedDatabaseHeaderV4`. `NativeReadViewSourceV1` owns the only captured-header immutable loader, yet its path/directory/FileRecord/chunk traversal is private and permission-specific. The separate `NativeIndexFileRevisionSourceV1` and maintenance scanner robustly read historical namespace-tree hashes for index tasks, but use `MemoryOwner::Task`/maintenance admission and direct v3 engine visibility; they cannot become an authorized query reader by relabeling their input.
- **Consumers and producers:** the new reader is produced only from an already-authorized `ResolvedReadViewV1` plus its originating native source. Consumers are P7-3 exact authoritative evaluation, P7-2 file-backed position-universe recomputation, P7-4 locators/range continuity, and selected-root permission reads. It returns exact FileKey/revision/path/FileRecord rows, bounded complete pages, bounded identity lookup outcomes, selected semantic definitions, and authoritative source values. P7-3c later supplies immutable index ArtifactDirectory/page access; P7-3d composes route-specific rows and boolean/order/aggregate execution.
- **Single-path and authority contract:** retain the exact captured header in the view and load every selected entity through `V4FirstAuthorityPublisher::load_immutable_entity_at_captured_header`; never recapture HEAD or substitute the inner namespace-tree hash for the public selected NamespaceRoot. Query work reserves `MemoryOwner::Query` with workload admission. Selected-root permissions must delegate their entity/path traversal to the same reader before b2 closes, while the mutation/index-maintenance source remains a distinct task authority for unpublished source roots. Every receipt binds database and physical identity, public NamespaceRoot, namespace-tree root, semantic root, publication sequence, and hash profile.
- **Bounds and failure direction:** page count, retained bytes, path bytes/depth, B-tree depth/nodes, identity-lookup documents/work, entity bytes, semantic definitions, and source-value output are explicit nonzero bounds. Absence is returned only after a complete exact lookup/scan; exhausted work is unavailable/resource-limited, never absence. Missing referenced entities, noncanonical directory/FileRecord representation, revision/path/FileKey mismatch, duplicate/out-of-order rows, dishonest completion, and selected-root/header drift are corruption. Cancellation and memory refusal remain distinct typed non-success outcomes and release reservations.
- **TDD and first falsifier:** first create an authorized historical view, advance HEAD, then ask the native reader for a late B-tree file and a FileKey/revision identity. The row must bind the old public NamespaceRoot and old revision while all reads remain below the old captured high-water. A reader that recaptures current HEAD, accepts the public root as a tree root, charges task memory, or reports an uncompleted identity scan as absent must fail the target. P7-3b1 freezes rows/pages/identity lookup; b2 delegates permissions; b3-b4 add exact selected semantic catalogs and source values; b5 closes both-width/failure/architecture proof.

## P7-3c Immutable Artifact Cursor Entry

- **Entry and split:** P7-3c enters at pushed green commit `f6f2b99`. P7-3c1 extracts one storage-neutral correctness-bearing ArtifactDirectory/page cursor and migrates the existing batch/compaction rank/key traversal onto it. P7-3c2 supplies one captured-header native source with query-memory accounting and exact selected-generation closure. P7-3c3 makes NVT a validated optional starting hint and closes fallback, bidirectional continuity, corruption, cancellation, pressure, and both-width proof.
- **Territory:** immutable page/directory bytes are produced by COW batch application and selected through exact FieldIndex/ValueStore/ScopeCatalog manifests. Current consumers are batch application, native compaction, NVT validation, scope-ordinal maintenance, and generation-closure checks; future consumers are exact/partial query execution, pagination/position universes, locators, and migration validation. Two overlapping walkers exist today: `index_batch_application` owns source-driven page ordinal/key traversal and neighbor validation, while `index_nvt` validates caller-materialized directory paths for key/PageId selection. The new cursor must become the shared walker rather than adding a third path.
- **Correctness boundary:** directory fences, aggregate ranks, and exact parent/child descriptors are authority. Posting PageIds and links are validated continuity metadata. Physical spans and NVT cells are hints only. Missing/corrupt correctness-bearing pages or directories never become an empty/complete result; absent, stale, corrupt, or pressure-limited NVT state falls back to exact directory traversal without changing answers.
- **Resources and cancellation:** every source read receives its remaining byte ceiling before allocation, depth and retained input are bounded, arithmetic is checked, cancellation is checked before and after source work, and native retained state is charged to `MemoryOwner::Query`. P7-3c adds no writer, route, service activation, persistent format, migration action, deployment, or production database access.
- **First falsifier:** build a two-level posting tree whose NVT hint names a structurally valid but stale page. A key/range seek must reject that hint, find the exact directory predecessor, and then traverse bidirectionally without omission or duplication. Corrupt either parent closure or one page link and the cursor must fail as corruption rather than report a complete scan.

## P7-3c1 Shared Artifact Cursor Completion

PageId ranges remain candidate hints because sibling ranges may overlap; bounded DFS resolves the exact leaf, rejects duplicate PageIds, and returns absence only after every candidate closes.

## P7-3d1 Authoritative Truth Executor Completion

- **Single truth path:** `query_executor` now owns one storage-neutral authoritative evaluator for a compiled selected-root query. It streams strict-FileKey document rows and nested canonical field values, validates complete scope and field receipts, reads each distinct field once per document, evaluates the complete frozen `eq`/`in`/ordered-range/contains/similar/phonetic/fuzzy/`match` vocabulary, composes `AND`/`OR`/`NOT`, and retains only exact FileKey/revision/path matches. Planning now carries the exact database hash algorithm into the compiled plan, and execution uses that sole authority instead of accepting a second caller-supplied profile.
- **Bounds and failure direction:** query result and semantic envelopes reserve `MemoryOwner::Query` before construction. Definition decoding, reusable compiled runtimes, folded text, canonical conversion, substring/fuzzy work, source rows, result capacity, counts, bytes, and work steps are bounded and cancellation-aware. Runtime/token allocation grows fallibly from observed input rather than reserving protocol maxima. Missing is a negative value, deterministic unindexability is typed historical unavailability, dishonest or malformed selected input is corruption, operational loss remains unavailable, and every failure releases all reservations. Field and document visitors latch their first error, so even a buggy source that discards callback failures cannot return partial success behind a complete receipt. Successful result memory remains retained until the result drops.
- **Adversarial scope correction:** final review found that valid field catalogs may carry different effective ScopeId sets while the initial executor used the first predicate's set. A red valid-plan regression observed an apparently successful answer that silently omitted one extra field scope. P7-3d1 now compares complete cross-field partitions before source work and returns `query_execution_scope_partition_unavailable` when they differ. P7-3d4 remains responsible for evaluating each effective scope independently and merging by FileKey; until then, this unit fails closed instead of exposing partial truth.
- **TDD and behavioral proof:** the first target failed to compile on the absent executor. Additional red-first checks caught retained-result overrun after two source reads, an unbounded shared Jaro-Winkler operation, the valid mixed-scope omission, and field/document sources that deliberately squelched callback failures. ## P7-3d2 Complete Candidate Execution Entry

- **Entry and split:** P7-3d2 enters at pushed green commit `87ca3ea`. P7-3d2a owns one storage-neutral bounded scanner over the shared ArtifactDirectory/page cursor: complete Posting constraints produce scope-local document ordinals, and the exact selected ScopeOrdinal tree resolves those ordinals to FileKey/revision/path identities. P7-3d2b adapts those identities into the P7-3d1 authoritative scope-source contract and requires a selected-root point recheck before any row reaches canonical predicate evaluation.
- **Authority boundary:** the compiled plan determines only which complete candidate generations and conservative coordinate/key regions may be scanned. Posting records are candidate hints, ScopeOrdinal records are immutable index identities, and neither is observable truth. Every candidate must be re-resolved under the exact selected NamespaceRoot and must retain the same FileKey, RecordRevision, and canonical path before the one authoritative evaluator may inspect values or emit a result. Missing/stale identities in a generation declared complete are corruption, not negative matches.
- **Expression and ordering contract:** `AND` may intersect complete supersets; `OR` must union every branch superset; `NOT` cannot complement a false-positive superset and therefore uses the complete live ScopeOrdinal universe before exact recheck. Candidate ordinals never cross ScopeId. Resolved rows are sorted and deduplicated by FileKey before entering the strict authoritative scope stream.
- **Bounds and failure direction:** candidate posting records, unique ordinals, resolved identities, cursor input, expression work, and selected-root rechecks have explicit count/byte/work limits and cancellation checks. Correctness-bearing Posting or ScopeOrdinal absence/corruption is terminal. Operational source loss, memory refusal, and cancellation remain typed; no source callback may squelch a visitor failure into a complete receipt.
- **First falsifiers:** P7-3d2a begins compile-red on the absent scanner and independently encoded Posting/ScopeOrdinal trees at both hash widths. It must prove exact point, all-postings intersection, conservative ordered range, tombstone handling, strict identity resolution, malformed closure, dishonest receipts, cancellation, and pressure. P7-3d2b then compares authoritative and complete-candidate results over the same selected-root corpus for `AND`, `OR`, and `NOT`, including false-positive postings and stale revision/path attempts.
